You will be assessed on this consistency.\n    14→\n    15→---\n    16→\n    17→## Project Context\n    18→\n    19→- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n    20→- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference paper and code repository. \n    21→- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n    22→- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n    23→\n    24→## Reference Information\n    25→\n    26→- The reference **paper** \"Separating cognitive and motor processes in the behaving mouse\" is in the file `paper.pdf`.\n    27→- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n    28→- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `code`.\n    29→- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `data`.\n    30→\n    31→## Decoder Task\n    32→\n    33→Temporally align based on **Go cue** onset. \n    34→\n    35→### Decoder Inputs:\n    36→- Time from go cue onset in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\n    37→\n    38→### Decoder Outputs\n    39→- **Lick direction** (left = 0, right = 1, per-trial)\n    40→- **Behavioral context** (WC = 0, DR = 1, per-trial)\n    41→- **Outcome** (incorrect = 0, correct = 1, per-trial)\n    42→- **Tongue velocity**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n    43→  - 0: < 50th percentile \n    44→  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n    45→- **Paw velocity**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n    46→  - 0: < 50th percentile \n    47→  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n    48→- **Motion energy**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n    49→  - 0: < 50th percentile \n    50→  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n    51→\n    52→## Target Data Format\n    53→\n    54→- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n    55→- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n    56→\n    57→```python\n    58→data = {\n    59→    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n    60→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    61→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n    62→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n    63→            ...\n    64→        ],\n    65→        [  # list of trials for session 2\n    66→            ...\n    67→        ],\n    68→        ...\n    69→    ],\n    70→\n    71→    'input': [  # list of sessions\n    72→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    73→            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n    74→            ...\n    75→        ],\n    76→        ...\n    77→    ],\n    78→\n    79→    'output': [  # list of sessions\n    80→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    81→            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n    82→            ...\n    83→        ],\n    84→        ...\n    85→    ],\n    86→\n    87→    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    88→    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n    89→\n    90→    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    91→    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n    92→        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    93→    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n    94→\n    95→    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    96→    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    97→    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n    98→        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n    99→        ...\n   100→    ],\n   101→\n   102→    'metadata': {\n   103→        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n   104→        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n   105→        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n   106→        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   107→        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   108→        ...\n   109→    }\n   110→}\n   111→```\n   112→\n   113→### Key Format Requirements\n   114→\n   115→- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n   116→  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n   117→  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n   118→  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n   119→\n   120→- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n   121→  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n   122→  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n   123→    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n   124→    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n   125→    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n   126→  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n   127→  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n   128→  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n   129→\n   130→- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n   131→  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n   132→  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n   133→  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n   134→  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n   135→  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n   136→\n   137→- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n   138→- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n   139→  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n   140→\n   141→- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n   142→- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n   143→\n   144→- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n   145→  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n   146→\n   147→- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n   148→  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n   149→- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n   150→  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n   151→\n   152→- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n   153→  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n   154→  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n   155→  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n   156→  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n   157→  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n   158→\n   159→## Python environment\n   160→\n   161→- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n   162→- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n   163→- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n   164→\n   165→## Decoder Reference\n   166→\n   167→- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n   168→- Optional arguments:\n   169→  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n   170→  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n   171→  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n   172→- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n   173→\n   174→### Purpose\n   175→\n   176→These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n   177→1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   178→2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   179→3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   180→4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n   181→5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n   182→\n   183→### Consistency requirements\n   184→\n   185→Your processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n   186→- Loading of data\n   187→- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n   188→- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n   189→- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n   190→\n   191→To check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n   192→\n   193→Whenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n   194→\n   195→Discrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n   196→\n   197→Carefully verify your work after every step. Data statistics and decoder accuracy will be compared to those achieved by human-written conversion code. \n   200→\n   201→---\n   202→\n   203→## Conversion Workflow\n   204→\n   205→**IMPORTANT**: Follow these steps in order. Experimental and behavioral variables\n   303→  - Data processing\n   304→  - Data curation\n   305→- Iterate until your understanding of the reference code, dataset, and reference texts are consistent\n   306→\n   307→**Done when**: You have resolved all discrepancies and documented your final understanding in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 4\".\n   308→\n   309→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 5, update Step 4 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   310→\n   311→---\n   312→\n   313→### Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   314→\n   315→**Goal**: Create a detailed plan for mapping source data to target format.\n   316→\n   317→**Actions**:\n   318→- Map source data elements to target format components:\n   319→  - Which variables contain neural activity?\n   320→  - Identify ALL available experimental variables\n   321→  - Which variables should be decoder **inputs** vs **outputs**? (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   322→  - How to discretize continuous outputs (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   323→  - Which inputs/outputs should be **time-varying** vs **per-trial** (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   324→  - How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\n   325→  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `code` directory. \n   326→- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\n   327→  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\n   328→  - Temporal alignment of trials\n   329→  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\n   330→  - Brain regions recorded from\n   331→- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\n   332→- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\n   333→- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\n   334→- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\n   335→\n   336→**Done when**:\n   337→- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \"Step 5\"\n   338→- You have documented all decisions under \"Step 5\"\n   339→- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \"Step 5\"\n   340→\n   341→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   342→\n   343→---\n   344→\n   345→### Step 6: Script Development\n   346→\n   347→**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `convert_data.py`.\n   348→\n   349→**Actions**:\n   350→- Write all conversion code in the file `convert_data.py`\n   351→- Script should run as **`python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\n   352→- Include options:\n   353→  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\n   354→  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\n   355→  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\n   356→- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\n   357→- Write efficient code:\n   358→  - Vectorize loops\n   359→  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\n   360→  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\n   361→  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\n   362→- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\n   363→  - There are no temporal misalignments\n   364→  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\n   365→- Write clear, well-documented functions\n   366→- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\n   367→- Validate data shapes and types at each step\n   368→- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\n   369→\n   370→**Done when**: `convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\n   371→\n   372→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   373→\n   374→---\n   375→\n   376→### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   377→\n   378→**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\n   379→\n   380→**Actions**:\n   381→1. Run `python -u convert_data.py sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee conversion_sample_out.txt`\n   382→  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\n   383→  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\n   384→  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \n   385→  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\n   386→2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\n   387→  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \n   388→  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \n   389→  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \n   390→3. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_sample_out.txt`.\n   391→4. Analyze the output file `verification_sample_out.txt`:\n   392→  a. Verify no errors reported.\n   393→  b. Attempt to address any warnings\n   394→  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\n   395→\n   396→**Done when**: \n   397→- `sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   398→- `verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   399→- Processing plots show no anomalies\n   400→- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 7\"\n   401→\n   402→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `sample_data.pkl` and `verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \n   403→\n   404→---\n   405→\n   406→### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n   407→\n   408→**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\n   409→\n   410→**Actions**:\n   411→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n   412→2. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   413→3. **Check accuracy**: Verify accuracy is above chance for EVERY output\n   414→\n   415→**Investigate any issues**:\n   416→- If accuracy is low: check for conversion bugs or reconsider output representation\n   417→\n   418→**Done when**: \n   419→- Decoder training completes without errors\n   420→- Loss decreases over epochs\n   421→- Accuracies are above chance\n   422→- Document all results in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 8\"\n   423→\n   424→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 9 until `train_decoder_sample_out.txt` file exists, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 8 is filled in. \n   425→\n   426→---\n   427→\n   428→### Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n   429→\n   430→**Goal**: Process the complete dataset.\n   431→\n   432→**Actions**:\n   433→1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\n   434→2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\n   435→3. Run `python -u convert_data.py converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee conversion_full_out.txt`\n   436→4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \n   437→5. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_full_out.txt`. \n   438→6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \n   439→7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\n   440→8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\n   441→9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\n   442→\n   443→**Done when**:\n   444→- `converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   445→- `verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   446→- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\n   447→- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 9\"\n   448→\n   449→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `converted_data.pkl` and `verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\n   450→\n   451→---\n   452→\n   453→### Step 10: Critical Review 1\n   454→\n   455→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\n   456→\n   457→**Check 1: Output log verification**\n   458→Read through `verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   459→\n   460→**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\n   461→- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\n   462→- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\n   463→- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \n   464→- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \n   465→- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \n   466→- Document your sanity checks to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   467→\n   468→**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\n   469→- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \n   470→- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \n   471→- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \n   472→- Fix any mismatches. \n   473→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   474→- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\n   475→\n   476→**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\n   477→- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference paper. \n   478→- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \n   479→- Fix any discovered issues. \n   480→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   481→\n   482→**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\n   483→- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \n   484→- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \n   485→\n   486→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   487→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   488→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   489→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   490→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   491→\n   492→Write a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 10\".\n   493→\n   494→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \n   495→\n   496→---\n   497→\n   498→### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n   499→\n   500→**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\n   501→\n   502→**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\n   503→\n   504→**Actions**:\n   505→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n   506→2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n   507→3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   508→4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the paper. \n   509→\n   510→**Done when**: \n   511→1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\n   512→2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 11\" with a table of accuracies\n   513→\n   514→---\n   515→\n   516→### Step 12: Critical Review 2\n   517→\n   518→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\n   519→\n   520→**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n   521→- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n   522→- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n   523→- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n   524→\n   525→**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to paper**\n   526→- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference paper\n   527→- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the paper's accuracy for each variable\n   528→- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n   529→- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n   530→\n   531→**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n   532→- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n   533→\n   534→**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n   535→1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n   536→2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n   537→3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n   538→4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n   539→5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and paper. \n   540→\n   541→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   542→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   543→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   544→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   545→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   546→\n   547→**Report all checks and results** to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \n   548→\n   549→**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 12\".\n   550→\n   551→---\n   552→\n   553→### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n   554→\n   555→**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\n   556→\n   557→**Actions**:\n   558→- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\n   559→  - Key decisions and rationale\n   560→  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\n   561→  - Validation results and tables\n   562→  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\n   563→- Create a user-facing `README.md` summarizing:\n   564→  - Dataset description\n   565→  - How to load and use the converted data\n   566→  - Output format specification\n   567→  - Key statistics\n   568→- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `cache/` folder\n   569→- Create `cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\n   570→\n   571→**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\n   572→\n   573→---\n   574→\n   575→## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\n   576→\n   577→**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. data\n   829→- **Trial alignment**: Align trials to meaningful events (stimulus onset, movement, etc.)\n   830→- **Time windows**: Choose appropriate pre/post-event windows\n   831→- **Neuron curation**: Choose filtering criteria for the quality of each neuron's recording based on reference texts\n   832→- **Trial curation**: Choose quality filtering criteria for trials based on reference texts or bad or missing data\n   833→\n   834→### Computational Efficiency\n   835→- Be mindful of memory usage with large datasets\n   836→- Use appropriate data types (float32 vs. float64)\n   837→- Consider lazy loading for very large datasets\n   838→- Implement efficient array operations (vectorization)\n   839→\n   840→## Success Criteria\n   841→\n   842→A successful conversion should:\n   843→1. Do this BEFORE exploring any code or data. Document everything in this file as you work.\n\n2. **FOLLOW STEPS IN ORDER**: Do not skip steps. Do not proceed to step N+1 until step N is complete. You will be assessed on this consistency.\n\n---\n\n## Project Context\n\n- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference paper and code repository. \n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n\n## Reference Information\n\n- The reference **paper** \"Separating cognitive and motor processes in the behaving mouse\" is in the file `paper.pdf`.\n- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `code`.\n- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `data`.\n\n## Decoder Task\n\nTemporally align based on **Go cue** onset. \n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n- Time from go cue onset in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n- **Lick direction** (left = 0, right = 1, per-trial)\n- **Behavioral context** (WC = 0, DR = 1, per-trial)\n- **Outcome** (incorrect = 0, correct = 1, per-trial)\n- **Tongue velocity**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n  - 0: < 50th percentile \n  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n- **Paw velocity**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n  - 0: < 50th percentile \n  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n- **Motion energy**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n  - 0: < 50th percentile \n  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n\nCarefully verify your work after every step.     continue\n   314→\n   315→        shifted_time = frame_times[valid] - vidshift - align_times[trial_idx]\n   316→        aligned[:, trial_idx] = np.interp(time_axis, shifted_time, me_trial[valid], left=np.nan, right=np.nan)\n   317→        aligned[:, trial_idx] = fill_nearest_1d(aligned[:, trial_idx])\n   318→\n   319→    return aligned\n   320→\n   321→\n   322→def select_valid_trials(obj: dict) -> np.ndarray:\n   323→    bp = obj[\"bp\"]\n   324→    early = ensure_1d_numeric(bp[\"early\"]) != 0\n   325→    no = ensure_1d_numeric(bp[\"no\"]) != 0\n   326→    hit = ensure_1d_numeric(bp[\"hit\"]) != 0\n   327→    miss = ensure_1d_numeric(bp[\"miss\"]) != 0\n   328→    stim_enable = ensure_1d_numeric(bp[\"stim\"][\"enable\"]) != 0\n   329→    valid = (~early) & (~no) & (~stim_enable) & (hit | miss)\n   330→    return np.flatnonzero(valid)\n   331→\n   332→\n   333→def get_first_lick_direction(obj: dict, trial_idx: int, go_time: float) -> int | None:\n   334→    lick_l = event_list_to_array(obj[\"bp\"][\"ev\"][\"lickL\"][trial_idx])\n   335→    lick_r = event_list_to_array(obj[\"bp\"][\"ev\"][\"lickR\"][trial_idx])\n   336→    lick_l = lick_l[lick_l >= go_time]\n   337→    lick_r = lick_r[lick_r >= go_time]\n   338→    first_l = lick_l[0] if lick_l.size else math.inf\n   339→    first_r = lick_r[0] if lick_r.size else math.inf\n   340→    if math.isinf(first_l) and math.isinf(first_r):\n   341→        return None\n   342→    return 0 if first_l < first_r else 1\n   343→\n   344→\n   345→def keep_quality(quality: str) -> bool:\n   346→    if quality is None:\n   347→        quality = \"\"\n   348→    quality = str(quality).strip()\n   349→    return quality not in QUALITY_EXCLUDE\n   350→\n   351→\n   352→def bin_unit_spikes(\n   353→    trialtm: np.ndarray,\n   354→    trial_numbers_1based: np.ndarray,\n   355→    align_times: np.ndarray,\n   356→    kept_trials_0based: np.ndarray,\n   357→) -> np.ndarray:\n   358→    trialtm = ensure_1d_numeric(trialtm)\n   359→    trial_numbers_1based = np.asarray(trial_numbers_1based, dtype=np.int64).reshape(-1)\n   360→\n   361→    local_map = np.full(align_times.size + 1, -1, dtype=np.int64)\n   362→    local_map[kept_trials_0based + 1] = np.arange(kept_trials_0based.size, dtype=np.int64)\n   363→\n   364→    spike_local_trial = local_map[trial_numbers_1based]\n   365→    valid_spikes = spike_local_trial >= 0\n   366→    aligned = trialtm[valid_spikes] - align_times[trial_numbers_1based[valid_spikes] - 1]\n   367→    spike_local_trial = spike_local_trial[valid_spikes]\n   368→    bin_idx = np.floor((aligned - TMIN) / DT).astype(np.int64)\n   369→    valid_bins = (bin_idx >= 0) & (bin_idx < TIME_AXIS.size)\n   370→    aligned_counts = np.zeros((kept_trials_0based.size, TIME_AXIS.size), dtype=np.float64)\n   371→    np.add.at(aligned_counts, (spike_local_trial[valid_bins], bin_idx[valid_bins]), 1.0)\n   372→    rates = aligned_counts / DT\n   373→    rates = my_smooth(rates.T, SMOOTH_N, BCTYPE).T\n   374→    return rates\n   375→\n   376→\n   377→def mean_firing_rate_window(\n   378→    trialtm: np.ndarray,\n   379→    trial_numbers_1based: np.ndarray,\n   380→    align_times: np.ndarray,\n   381→) -> float:\n   382→    trialtm = ensure_1d_numeric(trialtm)\n   383→    trial_numbers_1based = np.asarray(trial_numbers_1based, dtype=np.int64).reshape(-1)\n   384→    aligned = trialtm - align_times[trial_numbers_1based - 1]\n   385→    in_window = (aligned >= TMIN) & (aligned < TMAX)\n   386→    return float(np.sum(in_window) / (align_times.size * (TMAX - TMIN)))\n   387→\n   388→\n   389→def convert_session(spec: SessionSpec, make_plot: bool = False) -> dict:\n   390→    session_start = time.perf_counter()\n   391→    print(f\"[session] loading {spec.session_id}\")\n   392→    obj = mat73.loadmat(spec.data_path)[\"obj\"]\n   393→    me = load_motion_energy(spec)\n   394→\n   395→    bp = obj[\"bp\"]\n   396→    align_times = ensure_1d_numeric(bp[\"ev\"][ALIGN_EVENT])\n   397→    valid_trials = select_valid_trials(obj)\n   398→    if valid_trials.size < 2:\n   399→        raise RuntimeError(f\"{spec.session_id}: fewer than 2 valid trials after filtering\")\n   400→\n   401→    autowater = ensure_1d_numeric(bp[\"autowater\"])\n   402→    hit = ensure_1d_numeric(bp[\"hit\"])\n   403→\n   404→    trial_labels = []\n   405→    kept_trials = []\n   406→    for trial_idx in valid_trials:\n   407→        lick_dir = get_first_lick_direction(obj, int(trial_idx), align_times[trial_idx])\n   408→        if lick_dir is None:\n   409→            continue\n   410→        kept_trials.append(int(trial_idx))\n   411→        trial_labels.append(\n   412→            (\n   413→                lick_dir,\n   414→                0 if autowater[trial_idx] != 0 else 1,\n   415→                1 if hit[trial_idx] != 0 else 0,\n   416→            )\n   417→        )\n   418→\n   419→    kept_trials = np.asarray(kept_trials, dtype=np.int64)\n   420→    if kept_trials.size < 2:\n   421→        raise RuntimeError(f\"{spec.session_id}: fewer than 2 trials with valid lick direction\")\n   422→\n   423→    print(f\"[session] {spec.session_id} valid trials after all filters: {kept_trials.size}\")\n   424→\n   425→    clu = obj[\"clu\"][spec.probe_index]\n   426→    if clu is None:\n   427→        raise RuntimeError(f\"{spec.session_id}: selected probe {spec.probe_index + 1} has no neural data\")\n   428→\n   429→    unit_quality = [str(q).strip() if q is not None else \"\" for q in clu[\"quality\"]]\n   430→    quality_keep = np.asarray([keep_quality(q) for q in unit_quality], dtype=bool)\n   431→    initial_units = int(quality_keep.sum())\n   432→    print(f\"[session] {spec.session_id} units after quality filter: {initial_units}\")\n   433→\n   434→    neural_trials_by_unit = []\n   435→    kept_unit_indices = []\n   436→    for unit_idx, use_unit in enumerate(quality_keep):\n   437→        if not use_unit:\n   438→            continue\n   439→        mean_fr = mean_firing_rate_window(\n   440→            clu[\"trialtm\"][unit_idx],\n   441→            np.asarray(clu[\"trial\"][unit_idx], dtype=np.int64),\n   442→            align_times,\n   443→        )\n   444→        if mean_fr <= LOW_FR_HZ:\n   445→            continue\n   446→        trialdat = bin_unit_spikes(\n   447→            clu[\"trialtm\"][unit_idx],\n   448→            np.asarray(clu[\"trial\"][unit_idx], dtype=np.int64),\n   449→            align_times,\n   450→            kept_trials,\n   451→        )\n   452→        kept_unit_indices.append(unit_idx)\n   453→        neural_trials_by_unit.append(trialdat)\n   454→\n   455→    if len(neural_trials_by_unit) == 0:\n   456→        raise RuntimeError(f\"{spec.session_id}: no units survived filtering\")\n   457→\n   458→    neural_by_trial = np.stack(neural_trials_by_unit, axis=0).astype(np.float32)\n   459→    print(f\"[session] {spec.session_id} units after FR filter: {neural_by_trial.shape[0]}\")\n   460→\n   461→    vidshift = compute_vidshift(obj)\n   462→    tongue_x, tongue_y = align_feature_positions(obj, 0, \"tongue\", align_times, TIME_AXIS, vidshift)\n   463→    tongue_visible = np.isfinite(tongue_x) & np.isfinite(tongue_y)\n   464→    tongue_vx, tongue_vy = compute_velocity(tongue_x, tongue_y, \"tongue\")\n   465→    tongue_speed = np.sqrt(tongue_vx**2 + tongue_vy**2)\n   466→\n   467→    paw_speeds = []\n   468→    for paw_feat in (\"top_paw\", \"bottom_paw\"):\n   469→        paw_x, paw_y = align_feature_positions(obj, 1, paw_feat, align_times, TIME_AXIS, vidshift)\n   470→        paw_vx, paw_vy = compute_velocity(paw_x, paw_y, paw_feat)\n   471→        paw_speeds.append(np.sqrt(paw_vx**2 + paw_vy**2))\n   472→    paw_stack = np.stack(paw_speeds, axis=0)\n   473→    paw_counts = np.sum(np.isfinite(paw_stack), axis=0)\n   474→    paw_speed = np.full(paw_counts.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\n   475→    np.divide(np.nansum(paw_stack, axis=0), paw_counts, out=paw_speed, where=paw_counts > 0)\n   476→\n   477→    motion_energy = align_motion_energy(obj, me, align_times, TIME_AXIS)\n   478→\n   479→    tongue_visible_kept = tongue_visible[:, kept_trials]\n   480→    if np.any(tongue_visible_kept):\n   481→        tongue_threshold = float(np.nanpercentile(tongue_speed[:, kept_trials][tongue_visible_kept], 50))\n   482→    else:\n   483→        tongue_threshold = 0.0\n   484→    paw_threshold = float(np.nanpercentile(paw_speed[:, kept_trials], 50))\n   485→    me_threshold = float(np.nanpercentile(motion_energy[:, kept_trials], 50))\n   486→\n   487→    session_neural = []\n   488→    session_input = []\n   489→    session_output = []\n   490→\n   491→    for local_trial_idx, trial_idx in enumerate(kept_trials):\n   492→        lick_dir, context_label, outcome_label = trial_labels[local_trial_idx]\n   493→        neural_trial = neural_by_trial[:, local_trial_idx, :]\n   494→        time_input = TIME_AXIS[None, :].astype(np.float32)\n   495→        tongue_bin = ((tongue_speed[:, trial_idx] >= tongue_threshold) & tongue_visible[:, trial_idx]).astype(np.int64)\n   496→        output_trial = np.vstack(\n   497→            [\n   498→                np.full(TIME_AXIS.size, lick_dir, dtype=np.int64),\n   499→                np.full(TIME_AXIS.size, context_label, dtype=np.int64),\n   500→                np.full(TIME_AXIS.size, outcome_label, dtype=np.int64),\n   501→                tongue_bin,\n   502→                (paw_speed[:, trial_idx] >= paw_threshold).astype(np.int64),\n   503→                (motion_energy[:, trial_idx] >= me_threshold).astype(np.int64),\n   504→            ]\n   505→        )\n   506→        session_neural.append(neural_trial.astype(np.float32))\n   507→        session_input.append(time_input)\n   508→        session_output.append(output_trial)\n   509→\n   510→    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - session_start\n   511→    print(\n   512→        f\"[session] {spec.session_id} done in {elapsed:.2f}s | \"\n   513→        f\"trials={len(session_neural)} units={neural_by_trial.shape[0]}\"\n   514→    )\n   515→\n   516→    result = {\n   517→        \"session_id\": spec.session_id,\n   518→        \"subject\": spec.animal,\n   519→        \"neural\": session_neural,\n   520→        \"input\": session_input,\n   521→        \"output\": session_output,\n   522→        \"brain_region_idx\": np.zeros((neural_by_trial.shape[0],), dtype=np.int64),\n   523→        \"thresholds\": {\n   524→            \"tongue_velocity\": tongue_threshold,\n   525→            \"paw_velocity\": paw_threshold,\n   526→            \"motion_energy\": me_threshold,\n   527→        },\n   528→        \"summary\": {\n   529→            \"n_trials\": len(session_neural),\n   530→            \"n_units\": int(neural_by_trial.shape[0]),\n   531→            \"n_trials_dr\": int(sum(1 for _, c, _ in trial_labels if c == 1)),\n   532→            \"n_trials_wc\": int(sum(1 for _, c, _ in trial_labels if c == 0)),\n   533→            \"n_correct\": int(sum(1 for _, _, o in trial_labels if o == 1)),\n   534→            \"n_incorrect\": int(sum(1 for _, _, o in trial_labels if o == 0)),\n   535→            \"n_left\": int(sum(1 for l, _, _ in trial_labels if l == 0)),\n   536→            \"n_right\": int(sum(1 for l, _, _ in trial_labels if l == 1)),\n   537→        },\n   538→    }\n   539→\n   540→    if make_plot:\n   541→        create_processing_plot(\n   542→            spec=spec,\n   543→            result=result,\n   544→            tongue_speed=tongue_speed[:, kept_trials],\n   545→            paw_speed=paw_speed[:, kept_trials],\n   546→            motion_energy=motion_energy[:, kept_trials],\n   547→        )\n   548→\n   549→    return result\n   550→\n   551→\n   552→def create_processing_plot(\n   553→    spec: SessionSpec,\n   554→    result: dict,\n   555→    tongue_speed: np.ndarray,\n   556→    paw_speed: np.ndarray,\n   557→    motion_energy: print(f\"[plot] saved {out_path}\")\n   652→\n   653→\n   654→def build_dataset(converted_sessions: list[dict]) -> dict:\n   655→    subjects = sorted({sess[\"subject\"] for sess in \"Decoder predicts lick direction, behavioral context, outcome, and discretized \"\n   688→                \"tongue/paw/motion-energy movement variables from single-trial neural activity.\"\n   689→ print(f\"[summary] elapsed seconds: {elapsed:.2f}\")\n   723→    for sess in converted_sessions:\n   724→        s = sess[\"summary\"]\n   725→        print(\n   726→            \"[summary] \"\n calls `loadObjs`, then `processData` per session/probe, and concatenates dual-probe sessions. |\n    33→| `processData` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/processData.m` | PROCESSING | Runs the standard preprocessing sequence: trial selection, cluster selection, spike alignment, PSTH/single-trial extraction, low-FR filtering, baseline FR estimation. |\n    34→| `findTrials` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/findTrials.m` | CURATION | Evaluates logical trial-condition expressions against `obj.bp` fields and returns matching trial indices. |\n    35→| `findClusters` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/findClusters.m` | CURATION | Selects units by quality label. |\n    36→| `alignSpikes` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/alignSpikes.m` | PROCESSING | Aligns spike times to the chosen event (`goCue` by default; also supports `jawOnset`, `moveOnset`, `firstLick`, `lastLick`). |\n    37→| `getSeq` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/getSeq.m` | PROCESSING | Bins aligned spikes on a uniform time grid and generates smoothed PSTHs and single-trial neural matrices. |\n    38→| `removeLowFRClusters` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/removeLowFRClusters.m` | CURATION | Removes units whose mean firing rate across all trials is below the threshold. |\n    39→| `baselineFR` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/baselineFR.m` | PROCESSING | Computes pre-sample baseline firing-rate mean and variance for retained units. |\n    40→| `loadMotionEnergy` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/loadMotionEnergy.m` | LOADING | Loads motion-energy files, aligns/interpolates them to the neural time base using video timestamps and alignment event, then thresholds into movement periods. |\n    41→| `getKinematicsFromVideo` | `code/funcs/kinematics/getKinematicsFromVideo.m` | PROCESSING | Extracts per-trial DLC positions and velocities for selected body parts, aligned to the same session time axis. |\n    42→| `findVelocity` | `code/funcs/kinematics/findVelocity.m` | PROCESSING | Computes per-feature velocity traces from aligned positions, with special handling for tongue visibility gaps. |\n    43→| `getOutcome` | `code/funcs/getOutcome.m` | PROCESSING | Returns per-trial correct/error labels from `bp.hit`, setting ignored/no-response trials to `NaN`. |\n    44→\n    45→### Notes\n    46→- Reference pipeline entry point is `loadSessionData(meta, params)`, which first loads raw session `obj` structs, then processes each requested probe independently, and concatenates probes when a session has two ALM probes.\n    47→- The tutorial in `code/WorkingWithDataObjs.m` documents the raw session object fields: `obj.bp` (behavior/trials), `obj.clu` (sorted units), `obj.traj` (DLC trajectories), `obj.me` (motion energy for behavior-only sessions), and `obj.ex` (session metadata).\n    48→- Neural data are electrophysiology, not imaging. There is no delta-F/F computation anywhere in the reference loading path.\n    49→- Spike alignment defaults to `goCue`. In the tutorial, authors explicitly use `params.alignEvent = 'goCue'`.\n    50→- Neural binning is performed on a uniform time grid from `tmin` to `tmax` with bin width `dt`. In the tutorial example this is `dt = 1/100` s (10 ms), while `getDefaultParams.m` uses `dt = 1/200` s (5 ms); both use a `-2.5` to `2.5` s window around alignment.\n    51→- `getSeq` stores single-trial neural activity in `obj.trialdat{probe}` with shape `(time, neurons, trials)` after dividing spike counts by `dt` and smoothing with `mySmooth`.\n    52→- Smoothing is causal Gaussian in the tutorial (`params.smooth = 15`, `params.bctype = 'reflect'` in the worked example). This is part of the reference neural preprocessing.\n    53→- Unit filtering occurs in two stages:\n    54→  - quality filter via `findClusters` with `params.quality` (tutorial/default commonly uses `'all'`, with tutorial comments noting `garbage`/`noisy` are excluded from useful units),\n    55→  - firing-rate filter via `removeLowFRClusters`, which removes units with mean FR below threshold (`1 Hz` in tutorial example, `0.5 Hz` in defaults).\n    56→- Trial-condition logic in tutorial/default code distinguishes delayed-response versus water-cued context using `autowater`:\n    57→  - delayed-response: `~autowater`\n    58→  - water-cued: `autowater`\n    59→- Trial-condition logic also excludes stimulation and early-lick trials in the standard analyses via `~stim.enable` and `~early`.\n    60→- Motion energy is loaded from companion files and aligned to the same trial-centered time axis by interpolating DLC/video-timestamped traces onto `obj.time + params.advance_movement`, after subtracting the video offset and the chosen alignment event time.\n    61→- Kinematics are derived from DLC trajectories, producing x/y displacement and x/y velocity for each tracked feature from both views, then concatenated into a common `(time, trials, features)` representation.\n    62→- The reference decoding scripts (`NeuralChoiceDecoding.m`, `NeuralContextDecoding.m`) decode from `obj.trialdat`, confirming that single-trial smoothed firing rates are the intended neural representation rather than raw spikes.\n    63→\n    64→---\n    65→\n    66→## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n    67→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    68→\n    69→### Data Structure\n    70→- `data/` contains four session groups:\n    71→  - 141→### Processing Details\n   142→- Two tasks alternate within a session:\n   143→  - delayed-response (DR): sample tone (1.3 s), delay, then 10 ms auditory go cue.\n   144→  - water-cued (WC): no auditory cues; water delivered at a random port/time.\n   145→- Sessions in the two-context paradigm begin with approximately 100 DR trials, then alternate between WC and DR in blocks of 10-25 trials.\n   146→- Temporal reference events in the paper are context-dependent:\n   147→  - DR analyses are described relative to go cue.\n   148→  - WC analyses are described relative to water drop.\n   149→  - The user’s decoder task specifically requires alignment to go cue onset, so WC trials will need a consistent handling rule relative to their water timing while preserving the paper’s trial/event semantics.\n   150→- Early licks are excluded from analyses in WC and, more generally, early lick / ignore trials are omitted from behavioral analyses.\n   151→- Video processing:\n   152→  - two cameras at 400 Hz,\n   153→  - position and velocity computed for tracked features,\n   154→  - missing values filled with nearest available value for all features except tongue,\n   155→  - tongue angle and length computed from bottom camera.\n   156→- Motion energy processing:\n   157→  - framewise difference between medians of next 5 and previous 5 frames,\n   158→  - per-frame scalar defined as 99th percentile across pixels,\n   159→  - threshold chosen manually on a per-session basis at the separation between two modes of the motion-energy distribution.\n   160→- Electrophysiology:\n   161→  - ALM recordings with H2 or Neuropixels 1.0 probes,\n   162→  - raw voltages sampled at 25 kHz,\n   163→  - session inclusion required at least 10 units.\n   164→- Decoder methods stated in paper:\n   165→  - logistic regression for choice and context decoding from either kinematic features or single-trial neural activity,\n   166→  - equal numbers of correct left and right lick trials,\n   167→  - separate model at each time bin,\n   168→  - ridge regularization,\n   169→  - four-fold cross-validation with 30% held out for testing,\n   170→  - chance from shuffled labels.\n   171→- Prediction of CD projections from kinematics used lagged 5 ms bins (`B = 6` previous bins), which is relevant for understanding the reference temporal resolution but is distinct from the simpler decoder-validation task here.\n   172→\n   173→### Curation Steps\n   174→\n   175→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   176→- Spike sorting done with JRCLUST and/or Kilosort 3 plus manual curation.\n   177→- Well-isolated single units defined by manual inspection of ISI violations, separation from other units, and stationarity across session.\n   178→- Sessions included only if they had at least 10 units.\n   179→- For subspace alignment and some single-unit analyses: only well-isolated single units with firing rates >1 Hz.\n   180→- For other analyses: all units with firing rates >1 Hz.\n   181→\n   182→**Trial curation rules**:\n   183→- Early lick trials were omitted from analyses.\n   184→- Ignore / no-response trials were omitted from behavioral analyses.\n   185→- Behavioral inclusion criterion for analyzed sessions: at least 40 correct DR trials per direction and 20 correct WC trials per direction, excluding early and ignore trials.\n   186→- For choice/context decoding, equal numbers of correct left and right lick trials were used.\n   187→\n   188→### Decoders Trained\n   189→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   190→| Choice from `CDchoice` projections | AUC = 0.86 ± 0.11 across sessions |\n   191→| Choice from neural population activity | Strongly above shuffled labels in Fig. 3b; exact scalar not stated in text |\n   192→| Choice from kinematic features | Strongly above shuffled labels in Fig. 3b; exact scalar not stated in text |\n   193→| Context from neural population activity | Strongly above shuffled labels in Fig. 4b; exact scalar not stated in text |\n   194→| Context from kinematic features | Strongly above shuffled labels in Fig. 4b; exact scalar not stated in text |\n   195→\n   196→---\n   197→\n   198→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   199→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   200→\n   201→### Discrepancies Found\n   202→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n   203→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   204→| Overall dataset scope | Codebase contains loaders for neural sessions and separate behavior-only inhibition sessions; context analyses use figure-specific session loaders | `data/` contains 120 sessions total, including 75 behavior-only sessions with no `clu` | Paper neural analyses are much smaller curated subsets (for example, 12 two-context sessions; 25 DR sessions; 19 randomized-delay sessions) | Treat the raw `data/` directory as a superset. For the decoder conversion, exclude sessions without neural data and match the paper/code subset relevant to the requested outputs. |\n   205→| Two-context neural subset | `Figure8a_thru_c.m` loads 12 specific ALM sessions via `loadJEB6/JEB7/EKH1/EKH3/JGR2/JGR3/JEB19_ALMVideo` | Raw `Ephys_Behavior/` contains 25 fixed-delay ephys sessions, 22 of which contain both DR and WC trials | Paper reports “12 sessions, six mice, 522 units” for the two-context dataset | Use the 12-session figure-specific loader list as the reference subset for context analyses, then enforce paper/code curation (quality + FR filtering). Mouse-count wording in paper versus code session-loader list will be re-checked against final converted totals if needed. |\n   206→| Raw unit counts vs paper unit counts | Reference code filters units by quality and low FR before analysis | Raw session files contain many `garbage`, `multi`, `poor`, `fair`, etc. units; raw counts are far above paper totals | Paper reports 522 units for two-context, 1,651 for fixed-delay DR, 845 for randomized delay after curation | Use curation consistent with code/paper: exclude junk-quality units and require firing rate > 1 Hz for analysis-ready neural data. |\n   207→| Low-FR threshold | `getDefaultParams.m` sets `lowFR = 0.5`; tutorial and several figure scripts use `lowFR = 1` | Raw data include many low-quality / low-rate units | Paper states analyses used units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz | Resolve in favor of the paper and figure scripts: use a 1 Hz firing-rate threshold. |\n   208→| Base neural time bin | Tutorial example uses `dt = 1/100` (10 ms); `getDefaultParams.m` and paper methods indicate 5 ms internal bins in later analyses | Raw spikes are continuous event times | Paper methods explicitly mention 5 ms bins for lagged kinematic prediction; decoder scripts then aggregate to 75 ms | Resolve in favor of 5 ms base binning for initial alignment/processing, with any coarser binning derived afterward if needed. |\n   209→| Alignment event across contexts | Reference code commonly aligns to `goCue`; paper text describes DR relative to go cue and WC relative to water drop | Raw mixed-context files still contain a populated `bp.ev.goCue` field on WC trials; on inspected sessions it behaves like the WC action-triggering event (water-drop-aligned event) rather than being missing | User requires universal alignment to “Go cue onset” | Use `bp.ev.goCue` as the universal alignment field. For WC trials, the stored `goCue` appears to serve as the water-presentation-equivalent event, which resolves the cross-context alignment requirement. |\n   210→| Randomized-delay session count | Paper says 19 randomized-delay sessions, four mice | Raw folder contains 22 randomized-delay session files | Paper also requires session inclusion only if there are at least 10 units, plus later curation | Treat `RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior/` as a raw superset; later curation should reduce this toward the paper total. These sessions are less suitable for the requested context decoder because context does not vary there. |\n   211→| Behavior-only inhibition sessions | Code README marks “Video only” sessions as behavior only | `DelayInhibition_*` and `GoCueInhibition_*` files have no neural `clu`, only behavior/video/motion-energy data | Paper uses these sessions for optogenetic behavior analyses, not neural population decoding | Exclude these sessions from the converted neural decoder dataset because decoder inputs require neural activity. |\n   212→\n   213→Final understanding after reconciliation:\n   214→- The requested decoder dataset should be built from neural ALM sessions, not behavior-only inhibition sessions.\n   215→- Because the decoder outputs include behavioral context (`WC` vs `DR`), the most paper-consistent primary dataset is the two-context ALM subset loaded by the context-analysis scripts rather than the entire raw ephys archive.\n   216→- Neural preprocessing should follow the reference electrophysiology path: align spikes to `goCue`, bin on a common grid, smooth, exclude junk-quality units, and apply a 1 Hz firing-rate threshold.\n   217→- Behavioral context is represented by `autowater` in both paper and code (`~autowater` = DR, `autowater` = WC).\n   218→- The raw `goCue` field can be used for both DR and WC trials because WC trials still have a meaningful `goCue`-equivalent event recorded.\n   219→\n   220→---\n   221→\n   222→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   223→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   224→\n   225→### Variable Mapping\n   226→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   227→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   228→| `obj.clu{probe}[*].trialtm`, `obj.clu{probe}[*].trial`, `bp.ev.goCue` | `neural` | Bin spikes at 5 ms on a common window around aligned event; convert to firing rate by dividing by `dt`; smooth with reference-style Gaussian smoothing | `alignSpikes`, `getSeq`, `mySmooth` | Use only the ALM probe specified by the figure/session loader metadata. |\n   229→| Common aligned time axis | `input[0]` | Continuous time-from-go-cue vector repeated for every trial | Reference decoders train one model per time bin after alignment | Single decoder input requested by user. |\n   230→| First post-alignment lick side from `bp.ev.lickL` / `bp.ev.lickR` | `output[0]` | Per-trial binary constant over time: left = 0, right = 1 | Raw event times; paper/code use lick-port events and directional trial structure | Use actual behavioral lick direction, not instructed trial side; this is required by the user task. |\n   231→| `bp.autowater` | `output[1]` | Per-trial binary constant over time: WC = 0, DR = 1 | Trial conditions in reference code (`autowater` vs `~autowater`) | Exact context variable used throughout the paper/code. |\n   232→| `bp.hit`, `bp.miss` | `output[2]` | Per-trial binary constant over time: incorrect = 0, correct = 1 | `getOutcome` | Restrict to hit/miss trials; exclude ignore/no-response trials. |\n   233→| DLC kinematics for side-view tongue feature | `output[3]` | Time-varying binary trace from per-timepoint tongue-speed magnitude, thresholded at session median | `getKinematicsFromVideo`, `findVelocity` | Planned scalar = `sqrt(xvel^2 + yvel^2)` for `tongue_*_view1`. |\n   234→| DLC kinematics for bottom-view paw feature(s) | `output[4]` | Time-varying binary trace from per-timepoint paw-speed magnitude, thresholded at session median | `getKinematicsFromVideo`, `findVelocity` | Planned scalar = mean speed magnitude across `top_paw` and `bottom_paw` in view 2. |\n   235→| Motion-energy trace | `output[5]` | Time-varying binary trace from aligned motion-energy value, thresholded at session median | `loadMotionEnergy` | Use aligned motion-energy stream after interpolation onto neural time base. |\n   236→| Loader-session metadata (`anm`, selected ALM probe) | `subjects`, `subject_idx`, `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Normalize subject names to mouse IDs; normalize selected recording area to ALM | Session loader functions, `ex.probe.loc` | All kept neural sessions are ALM sessions from figure-specific loaders. |\n   237→\n   238→### Key Decisions\n   239→1. **Session subset**: Convert the paper’s two-context ALM ephys subset rather than every raw file. Rationale: the requested decoder includes behavioral context (`WC` vs `DR`), which only varies in the two-context neural sessions, and behavior-only inhibition sessions have no neural activity.\n   240→2. **Use figure-specific session list**: Use the 12-session context-analysis loader set (`JEB6`, `JEB7`, `EKH1`, `EKH3`, `JGR2`, `JGR3`, `JEB19`) as the reference inclusion set before neural curation. Rationale: this is the clearest code path corresponding to the paper’s context analyses.\n   241→3. **Trial inclusion**: Keep non-stim, non-early, non-ignore neural trials with a valid lick outcome (`hit` or `miss`). Rationale: this matches the paper’s omission of early/ignore trials and keeps outcome well-defined.\n   242→4. **Alignment**: Align every signal to `bp.ev.goCue`. Rationale: this is the requested decoder alignment, is the default in the reference code, and raw WC trials still contain a meaningful `goCue`-equivalent event.\n   243→5. **Neural representation**: Use smoothed single-trial firing rates rather than raw spike binaries. Rationale: the reference neural decoding scripts operate on `obj.trialdat`, which is the smoothed firing-rate representation.\n   244→6. **Neural curation**: Exclude junk-quality units and require firing rate > 1 Hz. Rationale: this matches the paper’s electrophysiology analysis criteria more closely than the looser 0.5 Hz default helper.\n   245→7. **Brain-region normalization**: Store region as `ALM` for all kept neurons. Rationale: the selected probe in the reference loader functions is the ALM probe; hemisphere-specific labels are less important than area identity for the target format.\n   246→8. **Lick direction definition**: Use actual first post-alignment lick side from lickport events, not the instructed side (`R`/`L`). Rationale: the user explicitly requested “lick direction”; for error trials this differs from the cue/target side.\n   247→9. **Static outputs as time-varying constants**: Represent lick direction, context, and outcome as constant binary traces across the full trial window. Rationale: this keeps all outputs in a uniform `(n_output, n_timepoints)` format while preserving per-trial labels.\n   248→10. **Continuous-movement discretization**: Compute per-session medians on the aligned traces across all kept trials/timepoints and bin each timepoint into `<50th percentile` vs `>=50th percentile`. Rationale: this follows the user’s required decoder-output discretization.\n   249→11. **Velocity scalar choice**: Reduce multi-axis DLC velocity to a scalar speed magnitude before thresholding. Rationale: the user asked for “velocity” as one output stream each for tongue and paw, and scalar speed is the most defensible collapse of x/y velocity.\n   250→12. **WC timing handling**: Treat stored WC `goCue` as the water-presentation-equivalent event already recorded in the raw data. Rationale: direct raw-data inspection showed populated `goCue` values on WC trials, making a unified alignment feasible without inventing a synthetic event.\n   251→\n   252→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   253→- [ ] Raw spike binning spot-check: for one kept session, neuron, and trial, compare converted binned firing-rate values against a direct histogram of raw `trialtm - goCue` spike times from the source file with `np.allclose()`.\n   254→- [ ] Lick-direction spot-check: for several trials, compare converted per-trial lick label against the earliest post-alignment value among raw `lickL` and `lickR` event times from the source file.\n   255→- [ ] Motion-energy alignment spot-check: compare converted aligned motion-energy trace for a chosen trial against direct interpolation from the raw motion-energy file using raw frame times and `goCue`, with `np.allclose()` after matching binning.\n   256→- [ ] Tongue/paw velocity spot-check: for selected trials/timepoints, compare converted speed traces against direct reconstruction from raw DLC positions and finite-difference velocity calculations using the same view/feature definitions.\n   257→- [ ] Session-statistics check: verify session counts, subject counts, and filtered neuron counts trend toward the paper’s 12-session / 522-unit two-context statistics after curation.\n   258→\n   259→---\n   260→\n   261→## Step 6: for the requested outputs are extracted (`tongue`, `top_paw`, `bottom_paw`) instead of the full feature set.\n   290→- Sample mode processes only the first two sessions to accelerate iteration.\n calls `loadObjs`, then `processData` per session/probe, and concatenates dual-probe sessions. |\n| `processData` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/processData.m` | PROCESSING | Runs the standard preprocessing sequence: trial selection, cluster selection, spike alignment, PSTH/single-trial extraction, low-FR filtering, baseline FR estimation. |\n| `findTrials` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/findTrials.m` | CURATION | Evaluates logical trial-condition expressions against `obj.bp` fields and returns matching trial indices. |\n| `findClusters` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/findClusters.m` | CURATION | Selects units by quality label. |\n| `alignSpikes` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/alignSpikes.m` | PROCESSING | Aligns spike times to the chosen event (`goCue` by default; also supports `jawOnset`, `moveOnset`, `firstLick`, `lastLick`). |\n| `getSeq` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/getSeq.m` | PROCESSING | Bins aligned spikes on a uniform time grid and generates smoothed PSTHs and single-trial neural matrices. |\n| `removeLowFRClusters` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/removeLowFRClusters.m` | CURATION | Removes units whose mean firing rate across all trials is below the threshold. |\n| `baselineFR` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/baselineFR.m` | PROCESSING | Computes pre-sample baseline firing-rate mean and variance for retained units. |\n| `loadMotionEnergy` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/loadMotionEnergy.m` | LOADING | Loads motion-energy files, aligns/interpolates them to the neural time base using video timestamps and alignment event, then thresholds into movement periods. |\n| `getKinematicsFromVideo` | `code/funcs/kinematics/getKinematicsFromVideo.m` | PROCESSING | Extracts per-trial DLC positions and velocities for selected body parts, aligned to the same session time axis. |\n| `findVelocity` | `code/funcs/kinematics/findVelocity.m` | PROCESSING | Computes per-feature velocity traces from aligned positions, with special handling for tongue visibility gaps. |\n| `getOutcome` | `code/funcs/getOutcome.m` | PROCESSING | Returns per-trial correct/error labels from `bp.hit`, setting ignored/no-response trials to `NaN`. |\n\n### Notes\n- Reference pipeline entry point is `loadSessionData(meta, params)`, which first loads raw session `obj` structs, then processes each requested probe independently, and concatenates probes when a session has two ALM probes.\n- The tutorial in `code/WorkingWithDataObjs.m` documents the raw session object fields: `obj.bp` (behavior/trials), `obj.clu` (sorted units), `obj.traj` (DLC trajectories), `obj.me` (motion energy for behavior-only sessions), and `obj.ex` (session metadata).\n- Neural data are electrophysiology, not imaging. There is no delta-F/F computation anywhere in the reference loading path.\n- Spike alignment defaults to `goCue`. In the tutorial, authors explicitly use `params.alignEvent = 'goCue'`.\n- Neural binning is performed on a uniform time grid from `tmin` to `tmax` with bin width `dt`. In the tutorial example this is `dt = 1/100` s (10 ms), while `getDefaultParams.m` uses `dt = 1/200` s (5 ms); both use a `-2.5` to `2.5` s window around alignment.\n- `getSeq` stores single-trial neural activity in `obj.trialdat{probe}` with shape `(time, neurons, trials)` after dividing spike counts by `dt` and smoothing with `mySmooth`.\n- Smoothing is causal Gaussian in the tutorial (`params.smooth = 15`, `params.bctype = 'reflect'` in the worked example). This is part of the reference neural preprocessing.\n- Unit filtering occurs in two stages:\n  - quality filter via `findClusters` with `params.quality` (tutorial/default commonly uses `'all'`, with tutorial comments noting `garbage`/`noisy` are excluded from useful units),\n  - firing-rate filter via `removeLowFRClusters`, which removes units with mean FR below threshold (`1 Hz` in tutorial example, `0.5 Hz` in defaults).\n- Trial-condition logic in tutorial/default code distinguishes delayed-response versus water-cued context using `autowater`:\n  - delayed-response: `~autowater`\n  - water-cued: `autowater`\n- Trial-condition logic also excludes stimulation and early-lick trials in the standard analyses via `~stim.enable` and `~early`.\n- Motion energy is loaded from companion files and aligned to the same trial-centered time axis by interpolating DLC/video-timestamped traces onto `obj.time + params.advance_movement`, after subtracting the video offset and the chosen alignment event time.\n- Kinematics are derived from DLC trajectories, producing x/y displacement and x/y velocity for each tracked feature from both views, then concatenated into a common `(time, trials, features)` representation.\n- The reference decoding scripts (`NeuralChoiceDecoding.m`, `NeuralContextDecoding.m`) decode from `obj.trialdat`, confirming that single-trial smoothed firing rates are the intended neural representation rather than raw spikes.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- `data/` contains four session groups:\n  - `Ephys_Behavior/`: standard delayed-response + water-cued sessions with electrophysiology, DLC trajectories, and companion `motionEnergy_*.mat` files.\n  - `RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior/`: randomized-delay electrophysiology sessions, also with companion `motionEnergy_*.mat` files.\n  - `DelayInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior/`: behavior-only bilateral motor-cortex inhibition sessions. These session files contain embedded `obj.me` motion-energy data and no `clu` field.\n  - `GoCueInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior/`: behavior-only go-cue inhibition sessions. These also contain embedded `obj.me` and no neural units.\n- Session files follow `data_structure_<animal>_<date>.mat`.\n- Motion-energy files follow `motionEnergy_<animal>_<date>.mat` for ephys sessions; behavior-only sessions store motion energy directly in the session file.\n- MATLAB file formats are mixed:\n  - many session files are MATLAB v7.3 / HDF5,\n  - some later session files are classic MAT files readable with `scipy.io.loadmat`,\n  - motion-energy files are classic MAT files.\n- Raw session object fields seen across files:\n  - ephys sessions: `obj.pth`, `obj.bp`, `obj.sglx`, `obj.traj`, `obj.trials`, `obj.clu`, `obj.ex`, and sometimes `obj.meta`\n  - behavior-only sessions: `obj.pth`, `obj.bp`, `obj.sglx`, `obj.traj`, `obj.trials`, `obj.me`, `obj.ex`\n- Behavioral/task fields in `obj.bp` include:\n  - trial labels: `R`, `L`, `hit`, `miss`, `no`, `early`, `autowater`, sometimes `autowaterBlock`, `autolearn`\n  - timing/event container `ev` with `bitStart`, `sample`, `delay`, `goCue`, `reward`, `lickL`, `lickR`\n  - stimulation/task metadata: `stim`, `protocol`, `bitRand`\n- DLC trajectories are stored in `obj.traj` for two camera views. Each trial struct contains `featNames`, `ts`, `frameTimes`, `NdroppedFrames`, and filename metadata.\n- Example tracked features:\n  - side view: `tongue`, `left_tongue`, `right_tongue`, `jaw`, `trident`, `nose`, `lickport`\n  - bottom view: `top_tongue`, `topleft_tongue`, `bottom_tongue`, `bottomleft_tongue`, `top_paw`, `bottom_paw`, `lickport`, `jaw`, `top_nostril`, `bottom_nostril`\n- Raw neural data are sorted spike trains per unit with fields such as `tm`, `trialtm`, `trial`, `quality`, `spkWavs`, and `site`/`channel`.\n- Raw unit quality labels are heterogeneous and include `garbage`, `multi`, `poor`, `fair`, `good`, `great`, plus capitalization variants.\n- No README or documentation files were found inside `data/`.\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic |
