units | “39% of single units” |\n\n\n### Processing Details\n- Two tasks alternate within a session:\n  - delayed-response (DR): sample tone (1.3 s), delay, then 10 ms auditory go cue.\n  - water-cued (WC): no auditory cues; water delivered at a random port/time.\n- Sessions in the two-context paradigm begin with approximately 100 DR trials, then alternate between WC and DR in blocks of 10-25 trials.\n- Temporal reference events in the paper are context-dependent:\n  - DR analyses are described relative to go cue.\n  - WC analyses are described relative to water drop.\n  - The user’s decoder task specifically requires alignment to go cue onset, so WC trials will need a consistent handling rule relative to their water timing while preserving the paper’s trial/event semantics.\n- Early licks are excluded from analyses in WC and, more generally, early lick / ignore trials are omitted from behavioral analyses.\n- Video processing:\n  - two cameras at 400 Hz,\n  - position and velocity computed for tracked features,\n  - missing values filled with nearest available value for all features except tongue,\n  - tongue angle and length computed from bottom camera.\n- Motion energy processing:\n  - framewise difference between medians of next 5 and previous 5 frames,\n  - per-frame scalar defined as 99th percentile across pixels,\n  - threshold chosen manually on a per-session basis at the separation between two modes of the motion-energy distribution.\n- Electrophysiology:\n  - ALM recordings with H2 or Neuropixels 1.0 probes,\n  - raw voltages sampled at 25 kHz,\n  - session inclusion required at least 10 units.\n- Decoder methods stated in paper:\n  - logistic regression for choice and context decoding from either kinematic features or single-trial neural activity,\n  - equal numbers of correct left and right lick trials,\n  - separate model at each time bin,\n  - ridge regularization,\n  - four-fold cross-validation with 30% held out for testing,\n  - chance from shuffled labels.\n- Prediction of CD projections from kinematics used lagged 5 ms bins (`B = 6` previous bins), which is relevant for understanding the reference temporal resolution but is distinct from the simpler decoder-validation task here.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Spike sorting done with JRCLUST and/or Kilosort 3 plus manual curation.\n- Well-isolated single units defined by manual inspection of ISI violations, separation from other units, and stationarity across session.\n- Sessions included only if they had at least 10 units.\n- For subspace alignment and some single-unit analyses: only well-isolated single units with firing rates >1 Hz.\n- For other analyses: all units with firing rates >1 Hz.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Early lick trials were omitted from analyses.\n- Ignore / no-response trials were omitted from behavioral analyses.\n- Behavioral inclusion criterion for analyzed sessions: at least 40 correct DR trials per direction and 20 correct WC trials per direction, excluding early and ignore trials.\n- For choice/context decoding, equal numbers of correct left and right lick trials were used.\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| Choice from `CDchoice` projections | AUC = 0.86 ± 0.11 across sessions |\n| Choice from neural population activity | Strongly above shuffled labels in Fig. 3b; exact scalar not stated in text |\n| Choice from kinematic features | Strongly above shuffled labels in Fig. 3b; exact scalar not stated in text |\n| Context from neural population activity | Strongly above shuffled labels in Fig. 4b; exact scalar not stated in text |\n| Context from kinematic features | Strongly above shuffled labels in Fig. 4b; exact scalar not stated in text |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution For WC trials, the stored `goCue` appears to serve as the water-presentation-equivalent event, which resolves the cross-context alignment requirement. |\n| Randomized-delay session count | Paper says 19 randomized-delay sessions, four mice | Raw folder contains 22 randomized-delay session files | Paper also requires session inclusion only if there are at least 10 units, plus later curation | Treat `RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior/` as a raw superset; later curation should reduce this toward the paper total. These sessions are less suitable for the requested context decoder because context does not vary there. |\n| Behavior-only inhibition sessions | Code README marks “Video only” sessions as behavior only | `DelayInhibition_*` and `GoCueInhibition_*` files have no neural `clu`, only behavior/video/motion-energy data | Paper uses these sessions for optogenetic behavior analyses, not neural population decoding | Exclude these sessions from the converted neural decoder dataset because decoder inputs require neural activity. |\n\nFinal understanding after reconciliation:\n- The requested decoder dataset should be built from neural ALM sessions, not behavior-only inhibition sessions.\n- Because the decoder outputs include behavioral context (`WC` vs `DR`), the most paper-consistent primary dataset is the two-context ALM subset loaded by the context-analysis scripts rather than the entire raw ephys archive.\n- Neural preprocessing should follow the reference electrophysiology path: align spikes to `goCue`, bin on a common grid, smooth, exclude junk-quality units, and apply a 1 Hz firing-rate threshold.\n- Behavioral context is represented by `autowater` in both paper and code (`~autowater` = DR, `autowater` = WC).\n- The raw `goCue` field can be used for both DR and WC trials because WC trials still have a meaningful `goCue`-equivalent event recorded.\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `obj.clu{probe}[*].trialtm`, `obj.clu{probe}[*].trial`, `bp.ev.goCue` | `neural` | Bin spikes at 5 ms on a common window around aligned event; exclude ignore/no-response trials. |\n| DLC kinematics for side-view tongue feature | `output[3]` | Time-varying binary trace from per-timepoint tongue-speed magnitude, thresholded at session median | `getKinematicsFromVideo`, `findVelocity` | Planned scalar = `sqrt(xvel^2 + yvel^2)` for `tongue_*_view1`. |\n| DLC kinematics for bottom-view paw feature(s) | `output[4]` | Time-varying binary trace from per-timepoint paw-speed magnitude, thresholded at session median | `getKinematicsFromVideo`, `findVelocity` | Planned scalar = mean speed magnitude across `top_paw` and `bottom_paw` in view 2. |\n| Motion-energy trace | `output[5]` | Time-varying binary trace from aligned motion-energy value, thresholded at session median | `loadMotionEnergy` | Use aligned motion-energy stream after interpolation onto neural time base. |\n| Loader-session metadata (`anm`, selected ALM probe) | `subjects`, `subject_idx`, `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Normalize subject names to mouse IDs; normalize selected recording area to ALM | Session loader functions, `ex.probe.loc` | All kept neural sessions are ALM sessions from figure-specific loaders. |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Session subset**: Convert the paper’s two-context ALM ephys subset rather than every raw file. Rationale: the requested decoder includes behavioral context (`WC` vs `DR`), which only varies in the two-context neural sessions, and behavior-only inhibition sessions have no neural activity.\n2. **Use figure-specific session list**: Use the 12-session context-analysis loader set (`JEB6`, `JEB7`, `EKH1`, `EKH3`, `JGR2`, `JGR3`, `JEB19`) as the reference inclusion set before neural curation. Rationale: this is the clearest code path corresponding to the paper’s context analyses.\n3. **Trial inclusion**: Keep non-stim, non-early, non-ignore neural trials with a valid lick outcome (`hit` or `miss`). Rationale: this matches the paper’s omission of early/ignore trials and keeps outcome well-defined.\n4. **Alignment**: Align every signal to `bp.ev.goCue`. Rationale: this is the requested decoder alignment, is the default in the reference code, and raw WC trials still contain a meaningful `goCue`-equivalent event.\n5. **Neural representation**: Use smoothed single-trial firing rates rather than raw spike binaries. Rationale: the reference neural decoding scripts operate on `obj.trialdat`, which is the smoothed firing-rate representation.\n6. **Neural curation**: Exclude junk-quality units and require firing rate > 1 Hz. Rationale: this matches the paper’s electrophysiology analysis criteria more closely than the looser 0.5 Hz default helper.\n7. **Brain-region normalization**: Store region as `ALM` for all kept neurons. Rationale: the selected probe in the reference loader functions is the ALM probe; hemisphere-specific labels are less important than area identity for the target format.\n8. **Lick direction definition**: Use actual first post-alignment lick side from lickport events, not the instructed side (`R`/`L`). Rationale: the user explicitly requested “lick direction”; for error trials this differs from the cue/target side.\n9. **Static outputs as time-varying constants**: Represent lick direction, context, and outcome as constant binary traces across the full trial window. Rationale: this keeps all outputs in a uniform `(n_output, n_timepoints)` format while preserving per-trial labels.\n10. **Continuous-movement discretization**: Compute per-session medians on the aligned traces across all kept trials/timepoints and bin each timepoint into `<50th percentile` vs `>=50th percentile`. Rationale: this follows the user’s required decoder-output discretization.\n11. **Velocity scalar choice**: Reduce multi-axis DLC velocity to a scalar speed magnitude before thresholding. Rationale: the user asked for “velocity” as one output stream each for tongue and paw, and scalar speed is the most defensible collapse of x/y velocity.\n12. **WC timing handling**: Treat stored WC `goCue` as the water-presentation-equivalent event already recorded in the raw data. Rationale: direct raw-data inspection showed populated `goCue` values on WC trials, making a unified alignment feasible without inventing a synthetic event.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Raw spike binning spot-check: for one kept session, neuron, and trial, compare converted binned firing-rate values against a direct histogram of raw `trialtm - goCue` spike times from the source file with `np.allclose()`.\n- [ ] Lick-direction spot-check: for several trials, compare converted per-trial lick label against the earliest post-alignment value among raw `lickL` and `lickR` event times from the source file.\n- [ ] Motion-energy alignment spot-check: compare converted aligned motion-energy trace for a chosen trial against direct interpolation from the raw motion-energy file using raw frame times and `goCue`, with `np.allclose()` after matching binning.\n- [ ] Tongue/paw velocity spot-check: for selected trials/timepoints, compare converted speed traces against direct reconstruction from raw DLC positions and finite-difference velocity calculations using the same view/feature definitions.\n- [ ] Session-statistics check: verify session counts, subject counts, and filtered neuron counts trend toward the paper’s 12-session / 522-unit two-context statistics after curation.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n[Implementation notes]\n- Created `convert_data.py` with CLI: `python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile> [--full|--sample] [--show-processing]`\n- Implemented the reference two-context ALM session subset directly from the behavior\n  - quality filtering consistent with `findClusters(..., {'all'})`\n  - low firing-rate filtering at 1 Hz\n  - motion-energy interpolation onto the neural time axis using video timestamps and video offset\n  - DLC-based tongue and paw speed extraction using reference-style position interpolation and velocity calculation\n- Implemented target-format assembly with:\n  - 1 time-varying simpler and reliable but not maximally lean.\n- Kinematic alignment currently interpolates each requested feature trial-by-trial in Python loops.\n\nCode speedups added:\n- Neural spike binning is vectorized within each unit using `np.add.at` rather than nested time-bin loops.\n- Only the exact kinematic features needed for the requested outputs are extracted (`tongue`, `top_paw`, `bottom_paw`) instead of the full feature set.\n- Sample mode processes only the first two sessions to accelerate iteration.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Sample calls `loadObjs`, then `processData` per session/probe, and concatenates dual-probe sessions. |\n    33→| `processData` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/processData.m` | PROCESSING | Runs the standard preprocessing sequence: trial selection, cluster selection, spike alignment, PSTH/single-trial extraction, low-FR filtering, baseline FR estimation. |\n    34→| `findTrials` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/findTrials.m` | CURATION | Evaluates logical trial-condition expressions against `obj.bp` fields and returns matching trial indices. |\n    35→| `findClusters` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/findClusters.m` | CURATION | Selects units by quality label. |\n    36→| `alignSpikes` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/alignSpikes.m` | PROCESSING | Aligns spike times to the chosen event (`goCue` by default; also supports `jawOnset`, `moveOnset`, `firstLick`, `lastLick`). |\n    37→| `getSeq` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/getSeq.m` | PROCESSING | Bins aligned spikes on a uniform time grid and generates smoothed PSTHs and single-trial neural matrices. |\n    38→| `removeLowFRClusters` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/removeLowFRClusters.m` | CURATION | Removes units whose mean firing rate across all trials is below the threshold. |\n    39→| `baselineFR` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/baselineFR.m` | PROCESSING | Computes pre-sample baseline firing-rate mean and variance for retained units. |\n    40→| `loadMotionEnergy` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/loadMotionEnergy.m` | LOADING | Loads motion-energy files, aligns/interpolates them to the neural time base using video timestamps and alignment event, then thresholds into movement periods. |\n    41→| `getKinematicsFromVideo` | `code/funcs/kinematics/getKinematicsFromVideo.m` | PROCESSING | Extracts per-trial DLC positions and velocities for selected body parts, aligned to the same session time axis. |\n    42→| `findVelocity` | `code/funcs/kinematics/findVelocity.m` | PROCESSING | Computes per-feature velocity traces from aligned positions, with special handling for tongue visibility gaps. |\n    43→| `getOutcome` | `code/funcs/getOutcome.m` | PROCESSING | Returns per-trial correct/error labels from `bp.hit`, setting ignored/no-response trials to `NaN`. |\n    44→\n    45→### Notes\n    46→- Reference pipeline entry point is `loadSessionData(meta, params)`, which first loads raw session `obj` structs, then processes each requested probe independently, and concatenates probes when a session has two ALM probes.\n    47→- The tutorial in `code/WorkingWithDataObjs.m` documents the raw session object fields: `obj.bp` (behavior/trials), `obj.clu` (sorted units), `obj.traj` (DLC trajectories), `obj.me` (motion energy for behavior-only sessions), and `obj.ex` (session metadata).\n    48→- Neural data are electrophysiology, not imaging. There is no delta-F/F computation anywhere in the reference loading path.\n    49→- Spike alignment defaults to `goCue`. In the tutorial, authors explicitly use `params.alignEvent = 'goCue'`.\n    50→- Neural binning is performed on a uniform time grid from `tmin` to `tmax` with bin width `dt`. In the tutorial example this is `dt = 1/100` s (10 ms), while `getDefaultParams.m` uses `dt = 1/200` s (5 ms); both use a `-2.5` to `2.5` s window around alignment.\n    51→- `getSeq` stores single-trial neural activity in `obj.trialdat{probe}` with shape `(time, neurons, trials)` after dividing spike counts by `dt` and smoothing with `mySmooth`.\n    52→- Smoothing is causal Gaussian in the tutorial (`params.smooth = 15`, `params.bctype = 'reflect'` in the worked example). This is part of the reference neural preprocessing.\n    53→- Unit filtering occurs in two stages:\n    54→  - quality filter via `findClusters` with `params.quality` (tutorial/default commonly uses `'all'`, with tutorial comments noting `garbage`/`noisy` are excluded from useful units),\n    55→  - firing-rate filter via `removeLowFRClusters`, which removes units with mean FR below threshold (`1 Hz` in tutorial example, `0.5 Hz` in defaults).\n    56→- Trial-condition logic in tutorial/default code distinguishes delayed-response versus water-cued context using `autowater`:\n    57→  - delayed-response: `~autowater`\n    58→  - water-cued: `autowater`\n    59→- Trial-condition logic also excludes stimulation and early-lick trials in the standard analyses via `~stim.enable` and `~early`.\n    60→- Motion energy is loaded from companion files and aligned to the same trial-centered time axis by interpolating DLC/video-timestamped traces onto `obj.time + params.advance_movement`, after subtracting the video offset and the chosen alignment event time.\n    61→- Kinematics are derived from DLC trajectories, producing x/y displacement and x/y velocity for each tracked feature from both views, then concatenated into a common `(time, trials, features)` representation.\n    62→- The reference decoding scripts (`NeuralChoiceDecoding.m`, `NeuralContextDecoding.m`) decode from `obj.trialdat`, confirming that single-trial smoothed firing rates are the intended neural representation rather than raw spikes.\n    63→\n    64→---\n    65→\n    66→## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n    67→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    68→\n    69→### Data Structure\n    70→- `data/` contains four session groups:\n    71→  - `Ephys_Behavior/`: standard delayed-response + water-cued sessions with electrophysiology, DLC trajectories, and companion `motionEnergy_*.mat` files.\n    72→  - `RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior/`: randomized-delay electrophysiology sessions, also with companion `motionEnergy_*.mat` files.\n    73→  - `DelayInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior/`: behavior-only bilateral motor-cortex inhibition sessions. These session files contain embedded `obj.me` motion-energy data and no `clu` field.\n    74→  - `GoCueInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior/`: behavior-only go-cue inhibition sessions. These also contain embedded `obj.me` and no neural units.\n    75→- Session files follow `data_structure_<animal>_<date>.mat`.\n    76→- Motion-energy files follow `motionEnergy_<animal>_<date>.mat` for ephys sessions; behavior-only sessions store motion energy directly in the session file.\n    77→- MATLAB file formats are mixed:\n    78→  - many session files are MATLAB v7.3 / HDF5,\n    79→  - some later session files are classic MAT files readable with `scipy.io.loadmat`,\n    80→  - motion-energy files are classic MAT files.\n    81→- Raw session object fields seen across files:\n    82→  - ephys sessions: `obj.pth`, `obj.bp`, `obj.sglx`, `obj.traj`, `obj.trials`, `obj.clu`, `obj.ex`, and sometimes `obj.meta`\n    83→  - behavior-only sessions: `obj.pth`, `obj.bp`, `obj.sglx`, `obj.traj`, `obj.trials`, `obj.me`, `obj.ex`\n    84→- Behavioral/task fields in `obj.bp` include:\n    85→  - trial labels: `R`, `L`, `hit`, `miss`, `no`, `early`, `autowater`, sometimes `autowaterBlock`, `autolearn`\n    86→  - timing/event container `ev` with `bitStart`, `sample`, `delay`, `goCue`, `reward`, `lickL`, `lickR`\n    87→  - stimulation/task metadata: `stim`, `protocol`, `bitRand`\n    88→- DLC trajectories are stored in `obj.traj` for two camera views. Each trial struct contains `featNames`, `ts`, `frameTimes`, `NdroppedFrames`, and filename metadata.\n    89→- Example tracked features:\n    90→  - side view: `tongue`, `left_tongue`, `right_tongue`, `jaw`, `trident`, `nose`, `lickport`\n    91→  - bottom view: `top_tongue`, `topleft_tongue`, `bottom_tongue`, `bottomleft_tongue`, `top_paw`, `bottom_paw`, `lickport`, `jaw`, `top_nostril`, `bottom_nostril`\n    92→- Raw neural data are sorted spike trains per unit with fields such as `tm`, `trialtm`, `trial`, `quality`, `spkWavs`, and `site`/`channel`.\n    93→- Raw unit quality labels are heterogeneous and include `garbage`, `multi`, `poor`, `fair`, `good`, `great`, plus capitalization variants.\n    94→- No README or documentation files were found inside `data/`.\n    95→\n    96→### Dataset Size (from data files)\n    97→| Statistic | Value |\n    98→|-----------|-------|\n    99→| Neurons (total) | 14,297 raw sorted units across neural sessions |\n   100→| Neurons / session | 317.71 mean across 45 sessions with neural data (range 17-1258) |"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01PA3N7rSbwfxPJzGng9TabP","session_id":"68e0bd42-95f2-4955-b67d-d24aa809f3f1","uuid":"ebf30b42-22f8-425f-a105-59b65806f8bd","timestamp":"2026-08-03T16:56:17.430Z"}

Statistics (from paper/methods)\n   124→| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n   125→|-----------|-------|--------------|\n   126→| Neurons (total) | 522 units in two-context ephys dataset | “12 ephys dataset | “12 sessions, six mice” |\n   129→| Sessions / subject | 2.0 mean in two-context ephys dataset | Derived from 12 sessions / 6 mice |\n   130→| Trials (total) | Not stated explicitly in text | Not explicitly given in paper/methods excerpt |\n   131→| Trials / session | Session starts with ~100 DR trials, then 10-25 trial alternating blocks | “approximately 100 DR trials” and “Each interleaved block was 10–25 trials” |\n   132→| Neural data time bin | 5 ms reference neural bin for CD prediction analyses | “B = 6; each bin is 5 ms” |\n   133→| Behavior data time bin | 400 Hz video = 2.5 ms/frame | “High-speed video was captured (400-Hz frame rate)” |\n   134→| Reward rate | Training criterion >70% accuracy on DR / randomized-delay tasks | “at least 70% accuracy” and “>70% accuracy” |\n   135→| Delay duration, standard DR | 0.9 s for 12 mice; 0.7 s for 1 mouse, linearly warped to 0.9 s | “0.9 s for 12 mice, 0.7 s for one mouse and linearly time warped to 0.9 s” |\n   136→| Randomized delay durations | 0.3, 0.6, 1.2, 1.8, 2.4, 3.6 s | “randomly selected from six possible values” |\n   137→| Choice-selective single units | 36% sample, 42% delay, 58% response of 483 single units | “sample: 36%; delay: 42%; response: 58%” |\n   138→| Context-selective single units | 39% of 214 single units | “39% of single units” |\n   139→\n   140→\n   141→### Processing Details\n   142→- Two tasks alternate within a session:\n   143→  - delayed-response (DR): sample tone (1.3 s), delay, then 10 ms auditory go cue.\n   144→  - water-cued (WC): no auditory cues; water delivered at a random port/time.\n   145→- Sessions in the two-context paradigm begin with approximately 100 DR trials, then alternate between WC and DR in blocks of 10-25 trials.\n   146→- Temporal reference events in the paper are context-dependent:\n   147→  - DR analyses are described relative to go cue.\n   148→  - WC analyses are described relative to water drop.\n   149→  - The user’s decoder task specifically requires alignment to go cue onset, so WC trials will need a consistent handling rule relative to their water timing while preserving the paper’s trial/event semantics.\n   150→- Early licks are excluded from analyses in WC and, more generally, early lick / ignore trials are omitted from behavioral analyses.\n   151→- Video processing:\n   152→  - two cameras at 400 Hz,\n   153→  - position and velocity computed for tracked features,\n   154→  - missing values filled with nearest available value for all features except tongue,\n   155→  - tongue angle and length computed from bottom camera.\n   156→- Motion energy processing:\n   157→  - framewise difference between medians of next 5 and previous 5 frames,\n   158→  - per-frame scalar defined as 99th percentile across pixels,\n   159→  - threshold chosen manually on a per-session basis at the separation between two modes of the motion-energy distribution.\n   160→- Electrophysiology:\n   161→  - ALM recordings with H2 or Neuropixels 1.0 probes,\n   162→  - raw voltages sampled at 25 kHz,\n   163→  - session inclusion required at least 10 units.\n   164→- Decoder methods stated in paper:\n   165→  - logistic regression for choice and context decoding from either kinematic features or single-trial neural activity,\n   166→  - equal numbers of correct left and right lick trials,\n   167→  - separate model at each time bin,\n   168→  - ridge regularization,\n   169→  - four-fold cross-validation with 30% held out for testing,\n   170→  - chance from shuffled labels.\n   171→- Prediction of CD projections from kinematics used lagged 5 ms bins (`B = 6` previous bins), which is relevant for understanding the reference temporal resolution but is distinct from the simpler decoder-validation task here.\n   172→\n   173→### Curation Steps\n   174→\n   175→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   176→- Spike sorting done with JRCLUST and/or Kilosort 3 plus manual curation.\n   177→- Well-isolated single units defined by manual inspection of ISI violations, separation from other units, and stationarity across session.\n   178→- Sessions included only if they had at least 10 units.\n   179→- For subspace alignment and some single-unit analyses: only well-isolated single units with firing rates >1 Hz.\n   180→- For other analyses: all units with firing rates >1 Hz.\n   181→\n   182→**Trial curation rules**:\n   183→- Early lick trials were omitted from analyses.\n   184→- Ignore / no-response trials were omitted from behavioral analyses.\n   185→- Behavioral inclusion criterion for analyzed sessions: at least 40 correct DR trials per direction and 20 correct WC trials per direction, excluding early and ignore trials.\n   186→- For choice/context decoding, equal numbers of correct left and right lick trials were used.\n   187→\n   188→### Decoders Trained\n   189→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   190→| Choice from `CDchoice` projections | AUC = 0.86 ± 0.11 across sessions |\n   191→| Choice from neural population activity | Strongly above shuffled labels in Fig. 3b; exact scalar not stated in text |\n   192→| Choice from kinematic features | Strongly above shuffled labels in Fig. 3b; exact scalar not stated in text |\n   193→| Context from neural population activity | Strongly above shuffled labels in Fig. 4b; exact scalar not stated in text |\n   194→| Context from kinematic features | Strongly above shuffled labels in Fig. 4b; exact scalar not stated in text |\n   195→\n   196→---\n   197→\n   198→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   199→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   200→\n   201→### Discrepancies Found\n   202→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n   203→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   204→| Overall dataset scope | Codebase contains loaders for neural sessions and separate behavior-only inhibition sessions; context analyses use figure-specific session loaders | `data/` contains 120 sessions total, including 75 behavior-only sessions with no `clu` | Paper neural analyses are much smaller curated subsets (for example, 12 two-context sessions; 25 DR sessions; 19 randomized-delay sessions) | Treat the raw `data/` directory as a superset. For the decoder conversion, exclude sessions without neural data and match the paper/code subset relevant to the requested outputs. |\n   205→| Two-context neural subset | `Figure8a_thru_c.m` loads 12 specific ALM sessions via `loadJEB6/JEB7/EKH1/EKH3/JGR2/JGR3/JEB19_ALMVideo` | Raw `Ephys_Behavior/` contains 25 fixed-delay ephys sessions, 22 of which contain both DR and WC trials | Paper reports “12 sessions, six mice, 522 units” for the two-context dataset | Use the 12-session figure-specific loader list as the reference subset for context analyses, then enforce paper/code curation (quality + FR filtering). Mouse-count wording in paper versus code session-loader list will be re-checked against final converted totals if needed. |\n   206→| Raw unit counts vs paper unit counts | Reference code filters units by quality and low FR before analysis | Raw session files contain many `garbage`, `multi`, `poor`, `fair`, etc. units; raw counts are far above paper totals | Paper reports 522 units for two-context, 1,651 for fixed-delay DR, 845 for randomized delay after curation | Use curation consistent with code/paper: exclude junk-quality units and require firing rate > 1 Hz for analysis-ready neural data. |\n   207→| Low-FR threshold | `getDefaultParams.m` sets `lowFR = 0.5`; tutorial and several figure scripts use `lowFR = 1` | Raw data include many low-quality / low-rate units | Paper states analyses used units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz | Resolve in favor of the paper and figure scripts: use a 1 Hz firing-rate threshold. |\n   208→| Base neural time bin | Tutorial example uses `dt = 1/100` (10 ms); `getDefaultParams.m` and paper methods indicate 5 ms internal bins in later analyses | Raw spikes are continuous event times | Paper methods explicitly mention 5 ms bins for lagged kinematic prediction; decoder scripts then aggregate to 75 ms | Resolve in favor of 5 ms base binning for initial alignment/processing, with any coarser binning derived afterward if needed. |\n   209→| Alignment event across contexts | Reference code commonly aligns to `goCue`; paper text describes DR relative to go cue and WC relative to water drop | Raw mixed-context files still contain a populated `bp.ev.goCue` field on WC trials; on inspected sessions it behaves like the WC action-triggering event (water-drop-aligned event) rather than being missing | User requires universal alignment to “Go cue onset” | Use `bp.ev.goCue` as the universal alignment field. For WC trials, the stored `goCue` appears to serve as the water-presentation-equivalent event, which resolves the cross-context alignment requirement. |\n   210→| Randomized-delay session count | Paper says 19 randomized-delay sessions, four mice | Raw folder contains 22 randomized-delay session files | Paper also requires session inclusion only if there are at least 10 units, plus later curation | Treat `RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior/` as a raw superset; later curation should reduce this toward the paper total. These sessions are less suitable for the requested context decoder because context does not vary there. |\n   211→| Behavior-only inhibition sessions | Code README marks “Video only” sessions as behavior only | `DelayInhibition_*` and `GoCueInhibition_*` files have no neural `clu`, only behavior/video/motion-energy data | Paper uses these sessions for optogenetic behavior analyses, not neural population decoding | Exclude these sessions from the converted neural decoder dataset because decoder inputs require neural activity. |\n   212→\n   213→Final understanding after reconciliation:\n   214→- The requested decoder dataset should be built from neural ALM sessions, not behavior-only inhibition sessions.\n   215→- Because the decoder outputs include behavioral context (`WC` vs `DR`), the most paper-consistent primary dataset is the two-context ALM subset loaded by the context-analysis scripts rather than the entire raw ephys archive.\n   216→- Neural preprocessing should follow the reference electrophysiology path: align spikes to `goCue`, bin on a common grid, smooth, exclude junk-quality units, and apply a 1 Hz firing-rate threshold.\n   217→- Behavioral context is represented by `autowater` in both paper and code (`~autowater` = DR, `autowater` = WC).\n   218→- The raw `goCue` field can be used for both DR and WC trials because WC trials still have a meaningful `goCue`-equivalent event recorded.\n   219→\n   220→---\n   221→\n   222→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   223→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   224→\n   225→### Variable Mapping\n   226→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   227→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   228→| `obj.clu{probe}[*].trialtm`, `obj.clu{probe}[*].trial`, `bp.ev.goCue` | `neural` | Bin spikes at 5 ms on a common window around aligned event; convert to firing rate by dividing by `dt`; smooth with reference-style Gaussian smoothing | `alignSpikes`, `getSeq`, `mySmooth` | Use only the ALM probe specified by the figure/session loader metadata. |\n   229→| Common aligned time axis | `input[0]` | Continuous time-from-go-cue vector repeated for every trial | Reference decoders train one model per time bin after alignment | Single decoder input requested by user. |\n   230→| First post-alignment lick side from `bp.ev.lickL` / `bp.ev.lickR` | `output[0]` | Per-trial binary constant over time: left = 0, right = 1 | Raw event times; paper/code use lick-port events and directional trial structure | Use actual behavioral lick direction, not instructed trial side; this is required by the user task. |\n   231→| `bp.autowater` | `output[1]` | Per-trial binary constant over time: WC = 0, DR = 1 | Trial conditions in reference code (`autowater` vs `~autowater`) | Exact context variable used throughout the paper/code. |\n   232→| `bp.hit`, `bp.miss` | `output[2]` | Per-trial binary constant over time: incorrect = 0, correct = 1 | `getOutcome` | Restrict to hit/miss trials; exclude ignore/no-response trials. |\n   233→| DLC kinematics for side-view tongue feature | `output[3]` | Time-varying binary trace from per-timepoint tongue-speed magnitude, thresholded at session median | `getKinematicsFromVideo`, `findVelocity` | Planned scalar = `sqrt(xvel^2 + yvel^2)` for `tongue_*_view1`. |\n   234→| DLC kinematics for bottom-view paw feature(s) | `output[4]` | Time-varying binary trace from per-timepoint paw-speed magnitude, thresholded at session median | `getKinematicsFromVideo`, `findVelocity` | Planned scalar = mean speed magnitude across `top_paw` and `bottom_paw` in view 2. |\n   235→| Motion-energy trace | `output[5]` | Time-varying binary trace from aligned motion-energy value, thresholded at session median | `loadMotionEnergy` | Use aligned motion-energy stream after interpolation onto neural time base. |\n   236→| Loader-session metadata (`anm`, selected ALM probe) | `subjects`, `subject_idx`, `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Normalize subject names to mouse IDs; normalize selected recording area to ALM | Session loader functions, `ex.probe.loc` | All kept neural sessions are ALM sessions from figure-specific loaders. |\n   237→\n   238→### Key Decisions\n   239→1. **Session subset**: Convert the paper’s two-context ALM ephys subset rather than every raw file. Rationale: the requested decoder includes behavioral context (`WC` vs `DR`), which only varies in the two-context neural sessions, and behavior-only inhibition sessions have no neural activity.\n   240→2. **Use figure-specific session list**: Use the 12-session context-analysis loader set (`JEB6`, `JEB7`, `EKH1`, `EKH3`, `JGR2`, `JGR3`, `JEB19`) as the reference inclusion set before neural curation. Rationale: this is the clearest code path corresponding to the paper’s context analyses.\n   241→3. **Trial inclusion**: Keep non-stim, non-early, non-ignore neural trials with a valid lick outcome (`hit` or `miss`). Rationale: this matches the paper’s omission of early/ignore trials and keeps outcome well-defined.\n   242→4. **Alignment**: Align every signal to `bp.ev.goCue`. Rationale: this is the requested decoder alignment, is the default in the reference code, and raw WC trials still contain a meaningful `goCue`-equivalent event.\n   243→5. **Neural representation**: Use smoothed single-trial firing rates rather than raw spike binaries. Rationale: the reference neural decoding scripts operate on `obj.trialdat`, which is the smoothed firing-rate representation.\n   244→6. **Neural curation**: Exclude junk-quality units and require firing rate > 1 Hz. Rationale: this matches the paper’s electrophysiology analysis criteria more closely than the looser 0.5 Hz default helper.\n   245→7. **Brain-region normalization**: Store region as `ALM` for all kept neurons. Rationale: the selected probe in the reference loader functions is the ALM probe; hemisphere-specific labels are less important than area identity for the target format.\n   246→8. **Lick direction definition**: Use actual first post-alignment lick side from lickport events, not the instructed side (`R`/`L`). Rationale: the user explicitly requested “lick direction”; for error trials this differs from the cue/target side.\n   247→9. **Static outputs as time-varying constants**: Represent lick direction, context, and outcome as constant binary traces across the full trial window. Rationale: this keeps all outputs in a uniform `(n_output, n_timepoints)` format while preserving per-trial labels.\n   248→10. **Continuous-movement discretization**: Compute per-session medians on the aligned traces across all kept trials/timepoints and bin each timepoint into `<50th percentile` vs `>=50th percentile`. Rationale: this follows the user’s required decoder-output discretization.\n   249→11. **Velocity scalar choice**: Reduce multi-axis DLC velocity to a scalar speed magnitude before thresholding. Rationale: the user asked for “velocity” as one output stream each for tongue and paw, and scalar speed is the most defensible collapse of x/y velocity.\n   250→12. **WC timing handling**: Treat stored WC `goCue` as the water-presentation-equivalent event already recorded in the raw data. Rationale: direct raw-data inspection showed populated `goCue` values on WC trials, making a unified alignment feasible without inventing a synthetic event.\n   251→\n   252→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   253→- [ ] Raw spike binning spot-check: for one kept session, neuron, and trial, compare converted binned firing-rate values against a direct histogram of raw `trialtm - goCue` spike times from the source file with `np.allclose()`.\n   254→- [ ] Lick-direction spot-check: for several trials, compare converted per-trial lick label against the earliest post-alignment value among raw `lickL` and `lickR` event times from the source file.\n   255→- [ ] Motion-energy alignment spot-check: compare converted aligned motion-energy trace for a chosen trial against direct interpolation from the raw motion-energy file using raw frame times and `goCue`, with `np.allclose()` after matching binning.\n   256→- [ ] Tongue/paw velocity spot-check: for selected trials/timepoints, compare converted speed traces against direct reconstruction from raw DLC positions and finite-difference velocity calculations using the same view/feature definitions.\n   257→- [ ] Session-statistics check: verify session counts, subject counts, and filtered neuron counts trend toward the paper’s 12-session / 522-unit two-context statistics after curation.\n   258→\n   259→---\n   260→\n   261→## Step 6: 64→\n    65→\n    66→def gaussian_window(n: int, alpha: float = 2.5) -> np.ndarray:\n    67→    if n <= 1:\n    68→        return np.ones((n,), dtype=np.float64)\n    69→    half = (n - 1) / 2.0\n Neuron Filtering (`findClusters`, `removeLowFRClusters`)\n\n**Quality Label Filtering** (`findClusters`):\n\n```python\nQUALITY_EXCLUDE = {\"garbage\", \"gabrga\", \"noisy\", \"real?\"}\nkeep_quality = [q not in QUALITY_EXCLUDE for q in unit_quality]\n```\n\n**Keep** quality labels (based on reference code comments):\n- `good`\n- `great`\n- `fair` \n- `poor`\n- `multi`\n- (and any other non-excluded labels)\n\n**Firing Rate Threshold** (`removeLowFRClusters`):\n- **Threshold**: `LOW_FR_HZ = 1.0` (1 Hz minimum mean firing rate)\n- Computed across all spikes in the alignment window: `[-2.5, 2.5]` seconds around go cue\n- Formula: `mean_fr = total_spikes_in_window / (n_trials * window_duration_sec)`\n\n**Result**: 519 neurons retained from two-context sessions (approximately 43.25 neurons/session)\n\n---\n\n### 4. Spike Alignment and Binning (`alignSpikes`, `getSeq`)\n\n**Alignment Event**:\n- **Default**: `bp.ev.goCue` (go-cue onset)\n- For both DR (delayed-response) and WC (water-cued) trials, uses the same `goCue` field\n- WC trials contain a meaningful `goCue`-equivalent event already recorded\n\n**Time Window**:\n```\ntmin = -2.5 seconds\ntmax = 2.5 seconds\ndt = 0.005 seconds (5 milliseconds)\nTIME_AXIS = np.arange(-2.5, 2.5, 0.005) + 0.0025  # 1000 bins\nEDGES = np.arange(-2.5, 2.5 + 0.005, 0.005)\n```\n\n**Binning Process** (from `bin_unit_spikes`):\n\n```python\n# For each unit:\n1. Get spike times (trialtm) and trial numbers (1-based)\n2. Align spike times: aligned = trialtm - align_times[trial_idx]\n3. Map to bin index: bin_idx = floor((aligned - tmin) / dt)\n4. Count spikes per bin per trial using np.add.at()\n5. Convert to firing rate: rates = spike_counts / dt\n6. Apply smoothing (see next section)\n```\n\n**Output Shape**: `(n_neurons, n_timepoints, n_trials)` → `(n_neurons, n_timepoints)` per trial after smoothing\n\n---\n\n### 5. Smoothing (`mySmooth`)\n\n**Smoothing Method**: Causal Gaussian convolution\n\n**Parameters**:\n```python\nSMOOTH_N = 15  # kernel size\nBCTYPE = \"reflect\"  # boundary condition type\n```\n\n**Gaussian Kernel**:\n```python\ndef gaussian_window(n: int, alpha: float = 2.5) -> np.ndarray:\n    half = (n - 1) / 2.0\n    k = np.arange(0, n, dtype=np.float64) - half\n    return np.exp(-0.5 * (alpha * k / half) ** 2)\n```\n\n**Causal Modification**:\n```python\n# Kernel is made causal by zeroing the future half:\nkern = gaussian_window(n)\nkern[: len(kern) // 2] = 0.0  # Zero out anti-causal part\nkern /= np.sum(kern)  # Normalize\n```\n\n**Boundary Handling**:\n- **\"reflect\"** mode: prepend n reflected copies of the signal before convolution, then trim\n- Allows smoothing all the way to the start of the window\n\n**Applied**: After spike binning, applied per-neuron across time for each trial\n\n---\n\n### 6. Kinematics Extraction (`getKinematicsFromVideo`, `findVelocity`)\n\n**Video Properties**:\n- **Frame rate**: 400 Hz (2.5 ms per frame)\n- **Views**: 2 camera views\n- **DLC trajectories**: `obj.traj[view_index]`\n\n**Position Alignment** (from `align_feature_positions`):\n\n```python\n# For each feature (e.g., \"tongue\", \"top_paw\", \"bottom_paw\"):\n1. Extract DLC positions (x, y) and frame times per trial\n2. Handle missing frame times: use nominal 400 Hz grid if absent\n3. Align frame times: shifted_time = frame_times - vidshift - align_times[trial_idx]\n4. Interpolate x, y onto neural time axis (1000 timepoints, 5 ms bins)\n5. For non-tongue features: apply nearest-neighbor fill to NaNs\n6. For tongue: leave NaNs as-is (visibility-aware)\n```\n\n**Velocity Computation** (from `compute_velocity`):\n\n```python\n# For each trial:\n1. Use np.gradient() to compute finite-difference velocities\n2. For non-tongue features:\n   - Subtract baseline (nanmedian of first diff) to remove drift\n   - Fill NaNs with nearest-neighbor interpolation\n3. For tongue:\n   - Keep NaNs as zero velocity where invisible\n   - No baseline subtraction (preserves zero in invisible periods)\n```\n\n**Used Features**:\n- **Tongue** (view 0): \"tongue\"\n- **Paw** (view 1): \"top_paw\" and \"bottom_paw\" (mean speed across both)\n\n**Output**: Speed magnitude\n```python\nspeed = sqrt(vx^2 + vy^2)\n```\n\n---\n\n### 7. Motion Energy Loading (`loadMotionEnergy`)\n\n**File Format**:\n- Classic MATLAB MAT file: `motionEnergy_<animal>_<date>.mat`\n- Contains `me` struct with fields:\n  - `me.data`: motion-energy time series per trial\n  - `me.moveThresh`: session-specific movement threshold\n\n**Alignment** (from `align_motion_energy`):\n\n```python\n# For each trial:\n1. Get frame times from DLC trajectory\n2. Load motion-energy values for that trial\n3. Align: shifted_time = frame_times - vidshift - align_times[trial_idx]\n4. Interpolate onto neural time axis (1000 timepoints, 5 ms bins)\n5. Fill NaNs with nearest-neighbor interpolation\n```\n\n**Video Shift Calculation** (from `compute_vidshift`):\n```python\nbit_start = mode_value(obj[\"bp\"][\"ev\"][\"bitStart\"])\nvid_file_offset = mode_value(obj[\"sglx\"][\"bitcode\"][\"bitstart\"]) / obj[\"sglx\"][\"fs\"]\nvidshift = vid_file_offset - bit_start\n```\n\n---\n\n### 8. Default Parameters (`getDefaultParams`)\n\n**Hard-coded Reference Parameters** in `convert_data.py`:\n\n```python\nDT = 0.005  # 5 ms bins\nTMIN = -2.5  # seconds before alignment event\nTMAX = 2.5  # seconds after alignment event\nSMOOTH_N = 15  # Gaussian kernel size\nBCTYPE = \"reflect\"  # Boundary condition\nLOW_FR_HZ = 1.0  # Firing rate threshold (Hz)\nALIGN_EVENT = \"goCue\"  # Alignment event field\nQUALITY_EXCLUDE = {\"garbage\", \"gabrga\", \"noisy\", \"real?\"}\n```\n\n---\n\n### 9. Outcomes (`getOutcome`)\n\n**Outcome Definition**:\n```python\nhit = ensure_1d_numeric(bp[\"hit\"])\noutcome = 1 if hit[trial_idx] != 0 else 0\n# outcome = 1 for correct (hit), 0 for incorrect (miss)\n```\n\n**Trial Filtering**:\n- Include only `hit == 1` or `miss == 1` trials\n- Exclude trials where outcome is undefined (`np.nan` or `no == 1`)\n\n---\n\n### 10. Behavioral Context Determination\n\n**Variable**: `bp.autowater`\n\n```python\ncontext = 0 if autowater[trial_idx] != 0 else 1\n# context = 0 for WC (water-cued, autowater=1)\n# context = 1 for DR (delayed-response, autowater=0)\n```\n\n**Paper-Consistent Definition**:\n- **Delayed-Response (DR)**: `~autowater` (no automatic water delivery)\n- **Water-Cued (WC)**: `autowater` (water delivered automatically at random port/time)\n\n---\n\n### 11. First Lick Direction\n\n**Definition** (from `get_first_lick_direction`):\n\n```python\nlick_l = event_list_to_array(bp[\"ev\"][\"lickL\"][trial_idx])\nlick_r = event_list_to_array(bp[\"ev\"][\"lickR\"][trial_idx])\nlick_l = lick_l[lick_l >= go_time]  # Post-alignment licks only\nlick_r = lick_r[lick_r >= go_time]\n\nfirst_l = lick_l[0] if lick_l.size else inf\nfirst_r = lick_r[0] if lick_r.size else inf\n\ndirection = 0 if first_l < first_r else 1\n# 0 = left lick, 1 = right lick\n```\n\n---\n\n### 12. Movement Discretization\n\n**Thresholding Strategy**: Per-session median split\n\n```python\n# For each movement output (tongue, paw, motion energy):\nthreshold = np.nanpercentile(movement_trace[:, kept_trials], 50)\nbinary_output = movement_trace >= threshold\n\n# Result: balanced binary output (≈50% low, ≈50% high by construction)\n```\n\n**Tongue Visibility Handling**:\n- Invisible periods identified: `~np.isfinite(tongue_x) | ~np.isfinite(tongue_y)`\n- Invisible periods always mapped to low bin (0)\n- Median threshold computed only on visible timepoints\n\n---\n\n## Summary Statistics: Trials** | 2,415 |\n| **Trials/session** | 201.25 |\n| **DR Trials** | ~1,719 (71%) |\n| **WC Trials** | ~696 (29%) |\n| **Correct Trials** | ~2,086 (86%) |\n| **Incorrect Trials** | ~329 (14%) |\n| **Temporal Alignment** | Go cue onset |\n| **Time Window** | [-2.5, 2.5] seconds |\n| **Neural Bin Width** | 5 milliseconds |\n| **Smoothing** | Causal Gaussian (n=15, α=2.5) |\n\n---\n\n## Key Implementation Notes\n\n1. **Spike binning is vectorized** using `np.add.at()` for efficiency\n2. **Alignment is unified**: both DR and WC trials use the same `goCue` field\n3. **Kinematics are For PSTHs, counts are divided by `numel(trix)` (number of trials) and by `dt`. For single-trial data, counts are divided by `dt` only (converting to firing rate in Hz). Boundary conditions: `'reflect'`, `'zeropad'`, or `'none'`.\n\n---\n\n### 5. `findVelocity.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction [xvel, yvel] = findVelocity(xpos, ypos, feat)\n\nfor i = 1:size(xpos,2) % for each trial\n    tsinterp = [xpos(:,i) ypos(:,i)];\n    basederiv = median(diff(tsinterp),'omitnan');\n\n    xvel(:,i) = gradient(tsinterp(:,1));\n    yvel(:,i) = gradient(tsinterp(:,2));\n\n    if ~contains(feat,'tongue')\n        xvel(:,i) = xvel(:,i) - basederiv(1);\n        yvel(:,i) = yvel(:,i) - basederiv(1);\n    end\n\n    if ~contains(feat,'tongue')\n        xvel(:,i) = fillmissing(xvel(:,i),'nearest');\n        yvel(:,i) = fillmissing(yvel(:,i),'nearest');\n    else\n        tempx = xvel(:,i);\n        tempx(isnan(tempx)) = 0;\n        xvel(:,i) = tempx;\n        tempy = yvel(:,i);\n        tempy(isnan(tempy)) = 0;\n        yvel(:,i) = tempy;\n    end\nend\nend\n```\n\nVelocity is computed using **`gradient`** (MATLAB equivalent of `np.gradient`, central differences), NOT `diff`. Note: Figure 8 scripts override `lowFR` to `1`.\n\n---\n\n### 2. `findClusters.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction idx = findClusters(qualityList, qualities)\n\nfor i = 1:numel(qualityList)\n    if ~ischar(qualityList{i}) && ~isstring(qualityList{i})\n        qualityList{i} = '';\n    end\nend\nqualityList = strtrim(qualityList);\n\nif any(strcmpi(qualities,'all')) || strcmpi(qualities{1},'all')\n    idx = find(~ismember(qualityList,'garbage') & ~ismember(qualityList,'gabrga') & ~ismember(qualityList,'noisy') & ~ismember(qualityList,'real?'));\n    return\nend\n\nfor i = 1:numel(qualityList)\n    if isempty(qualityList{i})\n        qualityList(i) = {'nan'};\n    end\nend\n\n[~,mask] = patternMatchCellArray(qualityList, qualities, 'any');\nidx = find(mask);\nend\n```\n\nWhen quality is `'all'`, it **excludes** clusters labeled `'garbage'`, `'gabrga'`, `'noisy'`, and `'real?'`.\n\n---\n\n### 3. `getSeq.m` (binning)\n\n```matlab\nfunction obj = getSeq(obj,params,prbnum,varargin)\n\nedges = params.tmin:params.dt:params.tmax;\nobj.time = edges + params.dt/2;\nobj.time = obj.time(1:end-1);\n\n% PSTH (trial-averaged):\nN = histc(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trialtm_aligned(spkix), edges);\nN = N(1:end-1);\nobj.psth{prbnum}(:,i,j) = mySmooth(N./numel(trix)./params.dt, params.smooth, params.bctype);\n\n% Single-trial data:\nN = histc(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trialtm_aligned(spkix), edges);\nN = N(1:end-1);\nobj.trialdat{prbnum}(:,i,j) = mySmooth(N./params.dt, params.smooth, params.bctype);\n```\n\nSpikes are binned with `histc` into edges from `tmin` to `tmax` at step `dt`. For PSTHs, counts are divided by `numel(trix)` (number of trials) and by `dt`. For single-trial data, counts are divided by `dt` only (converting to firing rate in Hz). Both are then smoothed with `mySmooth`.\n\n---\n\n### 4. `mySmooth.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction [out,kernsd] = mySmooth(x, N, varargin)\n% gaussian kernel with window size N\n% causal: first half of kernel zeroed out\n\nif N == 0 || N == 1\n    out = x; kernsd = 0; return\nend\n\nif isrow(x)\n    x = x';\nend\n\nif nargin > 2\n    bctype = varargin{1};\n    if strcmpi(bctype,'reflect')\n        x_filt = cat(1,x(1:N,:),x); trim = N + 1;\n    elseif strcmpi(bctype,'zeropad')\n        x_filt = cat(1,zeros(N,size(x,2)),x); trim = N + 1;\n    elseif strcmpi(bctype,'none')\n        x_filt = x; trim = 1;\n    else\n        x_filt = x; trim = 1;\n    end\nelse\n    x_filt = x; trim = 1;\nend\n\nkern = gausswin(N);\nkernsd = std(1:N);\n\nkern(1:floor(numel(kern)/2)) = 0; % CAUSAL: zero out first half\nkern = kern./sum(kern);\n\nout = zeros(Nel, Ncol);\nfor j = 1:Ncol\n    out(:, j) = conv(x_filt(:, j), kern, 'same');\nend\nout = out(trim:end,:);\nend\n```\n\nKey: uses `gausswin(N)` with the first half zeroed out to make it **causal**, then normalizes. Convolution with `'same'` mode. Boundary conditions: `'reflect'`, `'zeropad'`, or `'none'`.\n\n---\n\n### 5. `findVelocity.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction [xvel, yvel] = findVelocity(xpos, ypos, feat)\n\nfor i = 1:size(xpos,2) % for each trial\n    tsinterp = [xpos(:,i) ypos(:,i)];\n    basederiv = median(diff(tsinterp),'omitnan');\n\n    xvel(:,i) = gradient(tsinterp(:,1));\n    yvel(:,i) = gradient(tsinterp(:,2));\n\n    if ~contains(feat,'tongue')\n        xvel(:,i) = xvel(:,i) - basederiv(1);\n        yvel(:,i) = yvel(:,i) - basederiv(1);\n    end\n\n    if ~contains(feat,'tongue')\n        xvel(:,i) = fillmissing(xvel(:,i),'nearest');\n        yvel(:,i) = fillmissing(yvel(:,i),'nearest');\n    else\n        tempx = xvel(:,i);\n        tempx(isnan(tempx)) = 0;\n        xvel(:,i) = tempx;\n        tempy = yvel(:,i);\n        tempy(isnan(tempy)) = 0;\n        yvel(:,i) = tempy;\n    end\nend\nend\n```\n\nVelocity is computed using **`gradient`** (MATLAB equivalent of `np.gradient`, central differences), NOT `diff`. For **non-tongue** features, baseline drift (`basederiv = median(diff(...))`) is subtracted from velocity and NaNs are filled with nearest. For **tongue** features, baseline is NOT subtracted, and NaNs are set to 0.\n\n---\n\n### 6. `getKinematicsFromVideo.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction [kin,featLeg,nans] = getKinematicsFromVideo(obj,params)\n\nNvarsPerFeat = 4;   % xdisp\n        featMat{viewix}(:,:,featix*NvarsPerFeat-2) = tempy;     % ydisp\n        featMat{viewix}(:,:,featix*NvarsPerFeat-1) = xvel{viewix}(:,:,featix);\n        featMat{viewix}(:,:,featix*NvarsPerFeat) = yvel{viewix}(:,:,featix);\n    end\nend\n\nkin = cat(3, featMat{:});\n\n% Feature legend naming:\nfeatLeg{i*NvarsPerFeat-3} = [allfeats{i} '_xdisp_view' num2str(v(i))];\nfeatLeg{i*NvarsPerFeat-2} = [allfeats{i} '_ydisp_view' num2str(v(i))];\nfeatLeg{i*NvarsPerFeat-1} = [allfeats{i} '_xvel_view' num2str(v(i))];\nfeatLeg{i*NvarsPerFeat}   = [allfeats{i} '_yvel_view' num2str(v(i))];\n```\n\nIt also includes a `setTongueBaselinePosition` helper that replaces NaN tongue positions with the session-mean initial tongue position.\n\n---\n\n### 7. `loadMotionEnergy.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction me = loadMotionEnergy(obj,meta,params,datapth,varargin)\n\n% Loads motion energy .mat file matching meta.anm and meta.date\nfn = patternMatchCellArray({contents.name}',{'motionEnergy',meta.date},'all');\ntemp = load(fullfile(mepth,fn));\nme = temp.me;\n\nvidshift = findVideoOffset(obj);\n\n% Align to neural time axis via interpolation\ntaxis = obj.time + params.advance_movement;\nalignTimes = obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent);\nme.newdata = zeros(numel(obj.time),numel(me.data));\nfor trix = 1:numel(me.data)\n    me.newdata(:,trix) = interp1(obj.traj{1}(trix).frameTimes-vidshift-alignTimes(trix),me.data{trix},taxis);\nend\n\nme.data = me.newdata;\nme.data = fillmissing(me.data,'nearest');\nme.move = me.data > (me.moveThresh);\n```\n\nInterpolates per-trial motion energy from video frame times onto the aligned neural time axis. How are the data split into trials?\n\ni. Within each session, trials are identified by integer indices from 0 to `Ntrials-1`. Valid trials are selected via `select_valid_trials()`, then further filtered to require a valid first lick direction after the go cue. Each kept trial becomes one entry in the session's neural/input/output lists.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef select_valid_trials(obj: dict) -> np.ndarray:\n    bp = obj[\"bp\"]\n    early = ensure_1d_numeric(bp[\"early\"]) != 0\n    no = ensure_1d_numeric(bp[\"no\"]) != 0\n    hit = ensure_1d_numeric(bp[\"hit\"]) != 0\n    miss = ensure_1d_numeric(bp[\"miss\"]) != 0\n    stim_enable = ensure_1d_numeric(bp[\"stim\"][\"enable\"]) != 0\n    valid = (~early) & (~no) & (~stim_enable) & (hit | miss)\n    return np.flatnonzero(valid)\n\n# Further filtering for valid lick direction:\nfor trial_idx in valid_trials:\n    lick_dir = get_first_lick_direction(obj, int(trial_idx), align_times[trial_idx])\n    if lick_dir is None:\n        continue\n    kept_trials.append(int(trial_idx))\n```\n\niii. The AI documented this matches the paper's omission of early/ignore trials and ensures outcome is well-defined (hit or miss). The additional lick-direction filter ensures every trial has a meaningful lick direction output.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Trials are filtered by: (1) excluding early-lick trials (`early != 0`), (2) excluding no-response/ignore trials (`no != 0`), (3) excluding stimulation trials (`stim.enable != 0`), (4) requiring hit or miss outcome, and (5) requiring a valid first post-go-cue lick direction (left or right).\n\nii. (Same code as 1-d above)\n\niii. The AI's CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 5 Key Decision 3 states: \"Keep non-stim, non-early, non-ignore neural trials with a valid lick outcome (hit or miss). Rationale: this matches the paper's omission of early/ignore trials and keeps outcome well-defined.\"\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. Neural data is derived from `obj.clu[probe_index]` — specifically the spike times (`trialtm`), trial assignments (`trial`), and quality labels (`quality`) for each unit on the selected probe.\n\nii.\n```python\nclu = obj[\"clu\"][spec.probe_index]\n# Per unit:\nclu[\"trialtm\"][unit_idx]  # spike times relative to trial\nclu[\"trial\"][unit_idx]    # 1-based trial number for each spike\nclu[\"quality\"]            # quality label for each unit\n```\n\niii. The AI identified that the reference neural decoding scripts operate on `obj.trialdat`, which is derived from spike trains via binning and smoothing, confirming that sorted spike times are the raw source.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spike times are aligned to the go cue, binned into 5 ms bins over a [-2.5, 2.5] s window, converted to firing rates (counts/dt), and smoothed with a causal Gaussian kernel (window size 15, reflect boundary condition).\n\nii.\n```python\nDT = 0.005\nTMIN = -2.5\nTMAX = 2.5\nSMOOTH_N = 15\nBCTYPE = \"reflect\"\n\ndef bin_unit_spikes(...):\n    aligned = trialtm[valid_spikes] - align_times[trial_numbers_1based[valid_spikes] - 1]\n    bin_idx = np.floor((aligned - TMIN) / DT).astype(np.int64)\n    np.add.at(aligned_counts, (spike_local_trial[valid_bins], bin_idx[valid_bins]), 1.0)\n    rates = aligned_counts / DT\n    rates = my_smooth(rates.T, SMOOTH_N, BCTYPE).T\n    return rates\n\ndef my_smooth(x, n, bctype=\"none\"):\n    kern = gaussian_window(n)\n    kern[: len(kern) // 2] = 0.0  # causal: zero out first half\n    kern /= np.sum(kern)\n    out[:, col] = np.convolve(x_filt[:, col], kern, mode=\"same\")\n```\n\niii. The AI documented that this matches the reference `getSeq` function which bins spikes, divides by dt to get firing rates, and applies `mySmooth` with a causal Gaussian kernel.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Two-stage filtering: (1) quality label filtering excludes units labeled `garbage`, `gabrga`, `noisy`, or `real?`; (2) firing rate filtering excludes units with mean firing rate <= 1 Hz across the alignment window.\n\nii.\n```python\nQUALITY_EXCLUDE = {\"garbage\", \"gabrga\", \"noisy\", \"real?\"}\nLOW_FR_HZ = 1.0\n\ndef keep_quality(quality: str) -> bool:\n    return quality not in QUALITY_EXCLUDE\n\n# FR filter:\nmean_fr = mean_firing_rate_window(clu[\"trialtm\"][unit_idx], ...)\nif mean_fr <= LOW_FR_HZ:\n    continue\n```\n\niii. The AI noted that `findClusters` with `quality='all'` excludes exactly these four labels, and that Figure 8 scripts set `lowFR = 1` (overriding the default 0.5).\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. All neural data is aligned to `bp.ev.goCue`. Spike times are subtracted by the go cue time for each trial before binning.\n\nii.\n```python\nALIGN_EVENT = \"goCue\"\nalign_times = ensure_1d_numeric(bp[\"ev\"][ALIGN_EVENT])\n# In bin_unit_spikes:\naligned = trialtm[valid_spikes] - align_times[trial_numbers_1based[valid_spikes] - 1]\n```\n\niii. The AI documented that `goCue` is the default alignment in the reference code and that WC trials still contain a meaningful `goCue`-equivalent event in the raw data, making unified alignment feasible.\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. The temporal resolution is 5 ms (DT = 0.005 s), producing 1000 time bins over the [-2.5, 2.5] s window. No temporal rebinning is applied after the initial binning.\n\nii.\n```python\nDT = 0.005\nTIME_AXIS = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX, DT, dtype=np.float64) + DT / 2.0  # 1000 bins\n```\n\niii. The AI chose 5 ms based on `getDefaultParams.m` which sets `dt = 1/200` (5 ms), and the paper's methods which mention 5 ms bins for lagged kinematic prediction.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds* derived from?\n\ni. The input is derived from the time axis itself — the center of each 5 ms time bin relative to the go cue alignment event. No raw behavioral variable is used directly.\n\nii.\n```python\nTIME_AXIS = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX, DT, dtype=np.float64) + DT / 2.0\n# Per trial:\ntime_input = TIME_AXIS[None, :].astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. The AI stated this is a \"continuous time-from-go-cue vector repeated for every trial\" as requested by the decoder task specification.\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds*?\n\ni. The time axis is computed as bin centers: `arange(TMIN, TMAX, DT) + DT/2`. This gives 1000 values from -2.4975 to 2.4975 seconds. The same array is used for every trial, shaped as `(1, n_timepoints)`.\n\nii.\n```python\nTIME_AXIS = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX, DT, dtype=np.float64) + DT / 2.0\ntime_input = TIME_AXIS[None, :].astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. No complex processing — just constructing a uniform time grid centered at 0 (go cue).\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The input time axis is the same time grid used for neural spike binning, so alignment is inherent — both share the same `TIME_AXIS`.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Same TIME_AXIS used for spike binning and input:\nEDGES = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX + DT, DT, dtype=np.float64)\nTIME_AXIS = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX, DT, dtype=np.float64) + DT / 2.0\n```\n\niii. Documented as a deliberate design choice — the time input is simply the aligned time axis that all signals share.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick direction* derived from?\n\ni. Lick direction is derived from `bp.ev.lickL` and `bp.ev.lickR` — the timestamps of left and right lick port contacts — and `bp.ev.goCue` for the alignment time.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef get_first_lick_direction(obj, trial_idx, go_time):\n    lick_l = event_list_to_array(obj[\"bp\"][\"ev\"][\"lickL\"][trial_idx])\n    lick_r = event_list_to_array(obj[\"bp\"][\"ev\"][\"lickR\"][trial_idx])\n    lick_l = lick_l[lick_l >= go_time]\n    lick_r = lick_r[lick_r >= go_time]\n    first_l = lick_l[0] if lick_l.size else math.inf\n    first_r = lick_r[0] if lick_r.size else math.inf\n    if math.isinf(first_l) and math.isinf(first_r):\n        return None\n    return 0 if first_l < first_r else 1\n```\n\niii. The AI chose to use actual behavioral lick direction (first post-go-cue lick) rather than the instructed trial side (R/L), as the user explicitly requested \"lick direction.\"\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick direction*?\n\ni. For each trial, the first left and right lick events occurring at or after the go cue time are found. The direction of the earliest lick determines the label: left = 0, right = 1. Trials with no post-go-cue licks are excluded. The label is replicated as a constant across all time bins.\n\nii.\n```python\noutput_trial = np.vstack([\n    np.full(TIME_AXIS.size, lick_dir, dtype=np.int64),\n    ...\n])\n```\n\niii. The AI documented this as Key Decision 8: \"Use actual first post-alignment lick side from lickport events, not the instructed side (R/L).\"\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Behavioral context* derived from?\n\ni. Behavioral context is derived from `bp.autowater` — whether automatic water delivery was enabled for the trial.\n\nii.\n```python\nautowater = ensure_1d_numeric(bp[\"autowater\"])\n# Per trial:\ncontext_label = 0 if autowater[trial_idx] != 0 else 1\n```\n\niii. The AI documented that `autowater` is the exact context variable used throughout the paper/code: `autowater` = WC (water-cued), `~autowater` = DR (delayed-response).\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Behavioral context*?\n\ni. The `autowater` field is read as a numeric array. For each trial, if `autowater != 0`, context = 0 (WC); otherwise context = 1 (DR). The label is replicated as a constant across all time bins.\n\nii.\n```python\ncontext_label = 0 if autowater[trial_idx] != 0 else 1\nnp.full(TIME_AXIS.size, context_label, dtype=np.int64)\n```\n\niii. Documented as straightforward binary encoding matching the reference code's DR/WC distinction.\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Outcome* derived from?\n\ni. Outcome is derived from `bp.hit` — whether the animal responded correctly.\n\nii.\n```python\nhit = ensure_1d_numeric(bp[\"hit\"])\noutcome_label = 1 if hit[trial_idx] != 0 else 0\n```\n\niii. The AI noted this matches the reference `getOutcome` function, with trial filtering already ensuring only hit or miss trials are included.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Outcome*?\n\ni. For each kept trial (already filtered to hit or miss), if `hit != 0` then outcome = 1 (correct), else outcome = 0 (incorrect). The label is replicated as a constant across all time bins.\n\nii.\n```python\noutcome_label = 1 if hit[trial_idx] != 0 else 0\nnp.full(TIME_AXIS.size, outcome_label, dtype=np.int64)\n```\n\niii. Simple binary encoding. No additional processing beyond the trial-level filtering.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Tongue velocity* derived from?\n\ni. Tongue velocity is derived from DLC trajectories in `obj.traj[0]` (side camera view), specifically the `tongue` feature's x,y position time series (`ts[:, 0:2, feat_index]`) and frame timestamps (`frameTimes`), plus video offset information from `bp.ev.bitStart` and `sglx.bitcode.bitstart`.\n\nii.\n```python\ntongue_x, tongue_y = align_feature_positions(obj, 0, \"tongue\", align_times, TIME_AXIS, vidshift)\ntongue_vx, tongue_vy = compute_velocity(tongue_x, tongue_y, \"tongue\")\ntongue_speed = np.sqrt(tongue_vx**2 + tongue_vy**2)\n```\n\niii. The AI identified \"tongue\" from the side camera (view 0) as the relevant feature, matching the reference code's `traj_features` listing.\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Tongue velocity*?\n\ni. Processing steps: (1) Extract tongue x,y positions per trial from DLC trajectories. (2) Align frame times to the go cue via video offset subtraction. (3) Interpolate positions onto the 5 ms neural time axis. (4) No smoothing for tongue (skipped when feature name contains \"tongue\"). (5) Compute velocity using `np.gradient` (central differences). (6) For tongue, NaN velocities are set to 0 (no baseline drift subtraction). (7) Compute scalar speed as `sqrt(vx^2 + vy^2)`.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Position alignment (no smoothing for tongue):\nif \"tongue\" not in feat_name:\n    xy = my_smooth(xy, 1, \"reflect\")\n# Velocity computation:\nxv = np.gradient(tsinterp[:, 0])\nyv = np.gradient(tsinterp[:, 1])\nif \"tongue\" not in feat_name:\n    xv = xv - basederiv[0]\n    yv = yv - basederiv[0]\nelse:\n    xv = np.where(np.isfinite(xv), xv, 0.0)\n    yv = np.where(np.isfinite(yv), yv, 0.0)\ntongue_speed = np.sqrt(tongue_vx**2 + tongue_vy**2)\n```\n\niii. The AI followed the reference `findVelocity.m` logic: gradient-based velocity, no baseline subtraction for tongue, NaN-to-zero for invisible tongue periods.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Tongue velocity* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. The 50th percentile (median) of tongue speed is computed per session across all kept trials, but only from time points where the tongue is visible. The binary output is: 1 if speed >= threshold AND tongue is visible, 0 otherwise (including all invisible periods).\n\nii.\n```python\ntongue_visible_kept = tongue_visible[:, kept_trials]\nif np.any(tongue_visible_kept):\n    tongue_threshold = float(np.nanpercentile(tongue_speed[:, kept_trials][tongue_visible_kept], 50))\nelse:\n    tongue_threshold = 0.0\n# Per trial:\ntongue_bin = ((tongue_speed[:, trial_idx] >= tongue_threshold) & tongue_visible[:, trial_idx]).astype(np.int64)\n```\n\niii. The AI initially had issues with tongue discretization collapsing because invisible periods dominated the median. Fixed by computing the median only from visible timepoints and assigning invisible periods to the low bin (0).\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Tongue velocity* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Tongue positions are interpolated from video frame times onto the same 5 ms time axis used for neural data, after subtracting the video offset and go cue alignment time. This ensures temporal alignment with neural data.\n\nii.\n```python\nshifted_time = frame_times - vidshift - align_times[trial_idx]\nxpos[:, trial_idx] = np.interp(time_axis, shifted_time[valid], xy[valid, 0], left=np.nan, right=np.nan)\n```\n\niii. Same alignment procedure as the reference `findPosition` / `getKinematicsFromVideo` functions.\n\n## 8-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Paw velocity* derived from?\n\ni. The AI identified `top_paw` and `bottom_paw` from the bottom camera (view 1) as the relevant features, matching the reference code's `traj_features` listing.\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Paw velocity*?\n\ni. Processing steps for each paw feature: (1) Extract x,y positions per trial. (2) Apply `my_smooth(xy, 1, \"reflect\")` (effectively no smoothing since N=1). (3) Align to go cue via video offset. (4) Interpolate onto 5 ms time axis. (5) Fill NaN positions with nearest-neighbor interpolation. (6) Compute velocity via `np.gradient` with baseline drift subtraction. (7) Fill NaN velocities with nearest-neighbor. (8) Compute scalar speed. (9) Average speed across `top_paw` and `bottom_paw`.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Non-tongue position processing:\nxy = my_smooth(xy, 1, \"reflect\")  # effectively no-op\nxpos[:, trial_idx] = fill_nearest_1d(xpos[:, trial_idx])\n# Velocity:\nxv = np.gradient(tsinterp[:, 0])\nyv = np.gradient(tsinterp[:, 1])\nxv = xv - basederiv[0]\nyv = yv - basederiv[0]  # Note: reference bug replicated - uses basederiv[0] for both\nxv = fill_nearest_1d(xv)\nyv = fill_nearest_1d(yv)\n# Averaging:\npaw_speed = np.nanmean(paw_stack, axis=0)  # mean of top_paw and bottom_paw speeds\n```\n\niii. The AI replicated the reference code's velocity computation including the baseline drift subtraction bug (using `basederiv[0]` for both x and y velocity).\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Paw velocity* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. The 50th percentile of paw speed is computed per session across all kept trials and all timepoints. Binary output: 1 if speed >= threshold, 0 otherwise.\n\nii.\n```python\npaw_threshold = float(np.nanpercentile(paw_speed[:, kept_trials], 50))\n# Per trial:\n(paw_speed[:, trial_idx] >= paw_threshold).astype(np.int64)\n```\n\niii. This follows the user's required 50th percentile discretization. The resulting distribution is exactly 50/50 by construction.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Motion energy* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. (Same `align_motion_energy` function shown in 9-b)\n\niii. Uses the same temporal framework as neural and kinematic data.\n\n## 10. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Several strategies: (1) Missing/NaN frame timestamps are replaced with a nominal 400 Hz grid. (2) Missing tongue positions are left as NaN (tongue visibility tracked). (3) Missing non-tongue positions and velocities are filled with nearest-neighbor interpolation (`fill_nearest_1d`). (4) Missing motion energy values are filled with nearest-neighbor. (5) Trials with no valid post-go-cue lick are excluded. (6) Dropped video frames are detected and trials with all-NaN dropped frames are skipped.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Frame time fallback:\nif frame_times is None:\n    frame_times = (np.arange(ts.shape[0], dtype=np.float64) + 1.0) / 400.0\n\n# Nearest-neighbor fill for non-tongue:\ndef fill_nearest_1d(x):\n    x[~good] = np.interp(idx[~good], idx[good], x[good])\n\n# Tongue visibility:\ntongue_visible = np.isfinite(tongue_x) & np.isfinite(tongue_y)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented finding 2 raw trials with empty/all-NaN frameTimes and 527 raw trials with completely invisible tongue, confirming these edge cases are handled.\n\n## 11-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. Loading the large MATLAB v7.3/HDF5 session files via `mat73.loadmat()` and the trial-by-trial kinematic alignment loops (interpolating DLC positions for tongue, top_paw, and bottom_paw features). The unit-by-unit loop for spike binning and FR filtering could be partially vectorized.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Trial loop in align_feature_positions (lines 207-244):\nfor trial_idx in range(n_trials):\n    ...\n# Column loop in my_smooth (lines 98-99):\nfor col in range(x_filt.shape[1]):\n    out[:, col] = np.convolve(x_filt[:, col], kern, mode=\"same\")\n# Unit loop for spike binning (lines 436-453):\nfor unit_idx, use_unit in enumerate(quality_keep):\n    ...\n```\n\niii. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. Video offset (`compute_vidshift`) is computed twice per session — once in `convert_session` for kinematics, and once inside `align_motion_energy`. The code computes `me.moveThresh` (loaded from the motion energy file) but never uses it — the decoder task requires 50th percentile thresholding instead. Position smoothing for non-tongue features (`my_smooth(xy, 1, \"reflect\")`) is a no-op (N=1 returns input unchanged). How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Several strategies: (1) Missing/NaN frame timestamps are replaced with a nominal 400 Hz grid. (2) Missing tongue positions are left as NaN (tongue visibility tracked). (3) Missing non-tongue positions and velocities are filled with nearest-neighbor interpolation (`fill_nearest_1d`). (4) Missing motion energy values are filled with nearest-neighbor. (5) Trials with no valid post-go-cue lick are excluded. (6) Dropped video frames are detected and trials with all-NaN dropped frames are skipped.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Frame time fallback:\nif frame_times is None:\n    frame_times = (np.arange(ts.shape[0], dtype=np.float64) + 1.0) / 400.0\n\n# Nearest-neighbor fill for non-tongue:\ndef fill_nearest_1d(x):\n    x[~good] = np.interp(idx[~good], idx[good], x[good])\n\n# Tongue visibility:\ntongue_visible = np.isfinite(tongue_x) & np.isfinite(tongue_y)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented finding 2 raw trials with empty/all-NaN frameTimes and 527 raw trials with completely invisible tongue, confirming these edge cases are handled.\n\n## 11-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. Loading the large MATLAB v7.3/HDF5 session files via `mat73.loadmat()` and the trial-by-trial kinematic alignment loops (interpolating DLC positions for tongue, top_paw, and bottom_paw features). Total conversion for 12 sessions takes ~72 seconds.\n\nii.\n```python\nobj = mat73.loadmat(spec.data_path)[\"obj\"]  # Most expensive I/O\n# Kinematic alignment loops:\nfor trial_idx in range(n_trials):\n    xpos[:, trial_idx] = np.interp(time_axis, shifted_time[valid], xy[valid, 0], ...)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented ~7.4 s/session average, with I/O being the dominant cost.\n\n## 11-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n\ni. The trial-by-trial loops in `align_feature_positions`, `compute_velocity`, and `align_motion_energy` could potentially be vectorized, though the per-trial varying valid-sample masks make this challenging. The unit-by-unit loop for spike binning and FR filtering could be partially vectorized.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Trial loop in align_feature_positions (lines 207-244):\nfor trial_idx in range(n_trials):\n    ...\n# Column loop in my_smooth (lines 98-99):\nfor col in range(x_filt.shape[1]):\n    out[:, col] = np.convolve(x_filt[:, col], kern, mode=\"same\")\n# Unit loop for spike binning (lines 436-453):\nfor unit_idx, use_unit in enumerate(quality_keep):\n    ...\n```\n\niii.
